```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```


## UNIT 3 — Your environment: what exists and where

**Nivel:** A1.2 · **Escenario:** describes la arquitectura del sistema y tu entorno de trabajo a alguien que acaba de llegar.
**Meta comunicativa:** decir qué hay, cuánto hay y dónde está.
**Gramática:** *there is / there are* · artículos *a / an / the* · plurales · contables e incontables · cuantificadores · preposiciones de lugar.

### 1 · Input

> **YOU:** Let me give you an overview. There are three main services: the API gateway, the payments service and the notification worker.
> **MAYA:** Is there a shared database?
> **YOU:** There's one Postgres instance for payments and there's a separate one for notifications. There isn't a shared schema — that was a deliberate decision.
> **MAYA:** How much traffic is there?
> **YOU:** About two thousand requests per second at peak. There are some spikes on Fridays because of payroll.
> **MAYA:** Are there any tests?
> **YOU:** There are a lot of unit tests, but there aren't many integration tests. That's a problem. There's also very little documentation, unfortunately.
> **MAYA:** Where's the config?
> **YOU:** It's in the repo, under `config/`. The secrets are in Vault, not in the repo.

### 2 · Notice — *there is / there are*

Equivale a **hay**. La diferencia con el español es que el inglés **conjuga** según el número de lo que sigue.

| | Afirmativo | Negativo | Interrogativo |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Singular / incontable** | There **is** a bug. (*There's*) | There **isn't** a bug. | **Is there** a bug? |
| **Plural** | There **are** three bugs. | There **aren't** any bugs. | **Are there** any bugs? |

**Respuestas cortas:** *Is there a workaround? — Yes, there **is**. / No, there **isn't**.*

**Pasado:** *There **was** an outage yesterday.* · *There **were** two incidents last week.*

> ❌ *"Have three services."* ❌ *"It has three services."* (si quieres decir "hay") ✅ *"**There are** three services."*
> **Ojo:** *have* sí se usa cuando el sujeto **posee**: *The service **has** three endpoints.* La diferencia: *there is* introduce existencia; *have* expresa posesión.

### 3 · Notice — artículos *a / an / the*

El español y el inglés usan los artículos de forma **distinta**, y este es un error que sobrevive hasta C1. Tres reglas:

**Regla 1 — *a/an* = primera mención, uno cualquiera.** *the* = ya identificado, único o mencionado antes.

> *We found **a** bug in the checkout flow. **The** bug only happens on mobile.*
> (primera mención → *a*; segunda mención → *the*)

**Regla 2 — *a* ante sonido consonántico, *an* ante sonido vocálico.** Depende del **sonido**, no de la letra.

| *a* | *an* |
|---|---|
| a URL /ˌjuː-/ | an API /ˌeɪ-/ |
| a user /ˈjuː-/ | an error |
| a unit test | an issue |
| a UI component | an SQL query /ˌes-/ |
| a one-off script /wʌn/ | an hour /ˈaʊər/ |
| a JSON payload | an ID /ˌaɪˈdiː/ |
| a European client /ˌjʊr-/ | an MVP /ˌem-/ |
| a REST API | an S3 bucket /ˌes-/ |

> ❌ *an user* ❌ *an URL* ❌ *a error* ❌ *a API*

**Regla 3 — Cuándo el inglés NO usa artículo (y el español sí).**

| Sin artículo en inglés | Ejemplo | Español |
|---|---|---|
| Plurales generales | **Microservices** are hard. | **Los** microservicios son difíciles. |
| Incontables generales | **Documentation** matters. | **La** documentación importa. |
| Lenguajes y tecnologías | I write **Python**. | Escribo **en** Python. |
| Conceptos abstractos | **Performance** is a feature. | **El** rendimiento… |
| *last/next* + tiempo | **last sprint**, **next week** | **el** sprint pasado |
| Comidas, transporte | at **lunch**, by **car** | |
| Nombres de empresa | I work at **Google**. | |

> ❌ *The microservices are complex.* (si hablas de microservicios en general) ✅ *Microservices are complex.*
> Con *the* significas "esos microservicios concretos".

### 4 · Notice — plurales, contables e incontables

**Plurales regulares:** +*s*. Con -s/-sh/-ch/-x → +*es* (*branches, patches, boxes*). Consonante+y → *-ies* (*dependencies, queries, libraries*). -f/-fe → *-ves* (*shelves, lives*).

**Plurales irregulares que usarás:**

| Singular | Plural |
|---|---|
| person | **people** |
| child | children |
| man / woman | men / women |
| analysis | **analyses** /əˈnæləsiːz/ |
| basis | bases |
| criterion | criteria |
| datum | **data** (hoy se usa como incontable: *the data is…*) |
| index | **indices** o indexes |
| matrix | matrices |
| schema | **schemas** o schemata |
| foot | feet |
| tooth | teeth |

**Incontables (uncountable):** no llevan *-s* ni *a/an*, y van con verbo en **singular**.

| Incontable | ❌ No digas | ✅ Di |
|---|---|---|
| **information** | *informations* | some information, a piece of information |
| **feedback** | *feedbacks* | some feedback, a piece of feedback |
| **software** | *softwares* | software, a piece of software |
| **hardware** | *hardwares* | |
| **advice** | *advices* | some advice, **a piece of advice** |
| **research** | *researches* | some research |
| **work** | *works* (en este sentido) | a lot of work, **a job** |
| **progress** | *progresses* | some progress |
| **traffic** | | a lot of traffic |
| **documentation** | *documentations* | |
| **knowledge** | *knowledges* | |
| **training** | *trainings* (aunque se oye en corporativo) | training sessions |
| **staff** | | staff **are** / staff members |
| **equipment** | *equipments* | |
| **news** | | *The news **is** good.* (singular) |

### 5 · Notice — cuantificadores

| Cuantificador | Con contables | Con incontables |
|---|---|---|
| **many** | ✅ many bugs | ❌ |
| **much** (sobre todo en ?/-) | ❌ | ✅ much traffic |
| **a lot of / lots of** | ✅ | ✅ |
| **a few** = unos pocos (positivo) | ✅ a few tests | ❌ |
| **few** = pocos (negativo) | ✅ few tests (= casi ninguno) | ❌ |
| **a little** = un poco (positivo) | ❌ | ✅ a little time |
| **little** = poco (negativo) | ❌ | ✅ little documentation |
| **some** (afirmativas) | ✅ | ✅ |
| **any** (negativas y preguntas) | ✅ | ✅ |
| **no / none** | ✅ | ✅ |
| **enough** | ✅ | ✅ |
| **plenty of** | ✅ | ✅ |
| **a couple of** = dos o tres | ✅ | ❌ |

**Contraste crítico:**
- *There are **a few** tests.* → hay unos cuantos, es algo bueno.
- *There are **few** tests.* → hay pocos, es un problema.
- *We have **a little** time.* → tenemos algo de tiempo.
- *We have **little** time.* → tenemos poco tiempo, mal.

**Some vs any:**
- Afirmativo: *There are **some** issues.*
- Negativo: *There aren't **any** issues.*
- Pregunta: *Are there **any** issues?*
- Ofertas y peticiones (aunque sean preguntas): *Would you like **some** help?* · *Could I have **some** more time?*

### 6 · Preposiciones de lugar

| Prep. | Uso | Ejemplo técnico |
|---|---|---|
| **in** | dentro de un espacio cerrado | in the repo, in the database, in memory, in production, in the logs, in a meeting |
| **on** | sobre una superficie / conectado | on the server, on disk, on GitHub, on the main branch, on Slack, on the call, on my machine, on the team |
| **at** | un punto concreto | at line 42, at runtime, at the office, at 9 a.m., at peak |
| **under** | debajo / bajo condición | under `config/`, under load, under the hood |
| **between** | entre dos | between the services |
| **among** | entre varios | among the team |
| **next to / beside** | al lado de | |
| **inside / outside** | dentro/fuera | outside the VPC |
| **above / below** | por encima/debajo (nivel) | below the threshold |
| **across** | a través de, por todo | across all regions |
| **within** | dentro de (límite) | within the same transaction |

**Las cinco combinaciones que se memorizan enteras:**
*in production* · *on the branch* · *at runtime* · *under load* · *in the logs*

### 7 · Sound

**Foco:** *there* /ðer/ vs *their* /ðer/ vs *they're* /ðer/ — se pronuncian **igual**. Y el contraste con *the* /ðə/.

| Palabra | IPA | Significado |
|---|---|---|
| there | /ðer/ | allí / hay |
| their | /ðer/ | su (de ellos) |
| they're | /ðer/ | ellos son/están |
| the | /ðə/ o /ðiː/ | el/la |
| this | /ðɪs/ | este |
| these | /ðiːz/ | estos |
| that | /ðæt/ | ese |
| those | /ðoʊz/ | esos |

> **Práctica de /ð/ + contraste /ɪ/-/iː/:** *"This is the thing: these three services are there, but their config isn't."*

*There's* se contrae en habla incluso ante plurales (*"there's three of them"*) — muy común oralmente, pero **escríbelo correctamente**: *there are three*.

### 8 · Chunks

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **Let me give you an overview.** | abrir una explicación |
| **There are three main components.** | enumerar arquitectura |
| **There isn't a shared database.** | negar existencia |
| **How much traffic is there?** | volumen |
| **Are there any tests?** | |
| **It's under `config/`.** | ubicación en repo |
| **at peak** / **under load** | condiciones |
| **requests per second (RPS)** | métrica |
| **That was a deliberate decision.** | justificar diseño |
| **It's a bit of a mess, to be honest.** | admitir deuda técnica |
| **There's room for improvement.** | eufemismo profesional |

### 9 · Practice

**Ejercicio 3A — *there is* / *there are* / *there isn't* / *there aren't*.**
1. ____ two open incidents right now.
2. ____ any documentation for this endpoint?
3. ____ a workaround, but it's ugly.
4. ____ enough test coverage. We need more.
5. How many services ____ in total?
6. ____ a lot of traffic on Fridays.
7. Last week ____ an outage. *(pasado)*

**Ejercicio 3B — *a*, *an*, *the* o **∅** (sin artículo).**
1. We found ____ bug. ____ bug only happens on mobile.
2. ____ microservices are harder than people think.
3. He's ____ SRE at ____ Amazon.
4. Can you send me ____ URL?
5. I need ____ hour to finish ____ migration.
6. ____ documentation is out of date.
7. She wrote ____ script that parses ____ logs.
8. This is ____ MVP, not ____ final product.
9. I work with ____ Python and ____ Go.
10. It's ____ one-line change.

**Ejercicio 3C — *much*, *many*, *a few*, *few*, *a little*, *little*.**
1. There isn't ____ documentation.
2. There are ____ integration tests — only three, actually. *(negativo)*
3. We have ____ time left, so let's start. *(positivo, algo de)*
4. How ____ services do you own?
5. There were ____ people in the meeting. *(unos cuantos)*
6. I don't have ____ experience with Kafka.
7. We have ____ time. We need to cut scope. *(negativo, poco)*

**Ejercicio 3D — Corrige.**
1. *There are a lot of informations in the logs.*
2. *I have three feedbacks for you.*
3. *The people is waiting.*
4. *Have two services in production.*
5. *I need an advice.*
6. *There is many bugs.*
7. *He gave me some advices about the architecture.*
8. *We use the Python.*
9. *It's in the main branch.* *(preposición)*
10. *The data are corrupted.* *(uso moderno)*

**Ejercicio 3E — Traduce.**
1. Hay tres servicios principales.
2. No hay documentación para este endpoint.
3. ¿Cuántas peticiones por segundo hay en el pico?
4. Hay muy pocos tests de integración.
5. La configuración está en el repo, bajo `config/`.
6. Los secretos no están en el repo.
7. Necesito una hora para terminar la migración.
8. Hay mucho tráfico los viernes.

### 10 · Challenge

**Reto 5.** Escribe una descripción de la arquitectura de tu sistema real en 10–12 frases. Requisitos: cuatro usos de *there is/are*, tres cuantificadores distintos, cinco preposiciones distintas, y al menos un incontable usado correctamente.

**Reto 6 (oral).** Explica esa arquitectura en voz alta en 90 segundos, sin leer, como si un compañero nuevo acabara de entrar. Grábate. Cuenta cuántas veces dices *"there is"* con un plural detrás.

```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```


## UNIT 4 — What are you working on right now?

**Nivel:** A1.3 · **Escenario:** te preguntan tu estado actual en Slack, en un 1:1 o en el stand-up.
**Meta comunicativa:** distinguir lo que haces siempre de lo que estás haciendo ahora.
**Gramática:** presente continuo · contraste con el presente simple · verbos de estado.

### 1 · Input

> **PRIYA:** Hey, what are you working on at the moment?
> **YOU:** I'm looking into the timeout issue on the payments endpoint. It's taking longer than I expected.
> **PRIYA:** Are you making any progress?
> **YOU:** Some. I'm not seeing the error locally, so I'm adding logs to reproduce it in staging. Right now I'm waiting for the pipeline.
> **PRIYA:** Is anyone helping you?
> **YOU:** Tom's helping with the load test. He's running it as we speak.
> **PRIYA:** OK. And what's happening with the migration? Is it still blocked?
> **YOU:** Yes, we're still waiting for the DBA to approve the schema change. Nothing's moving until then.
> **PRIYA:** These days everything depends on that team. It's getting worse.

### 2 · Notice — presente continuo

**Estructura:** *to be* + verbo**-ing**.

| Afirmativo | Negativo | Interrogativo |
|---|---|---|
| I **am working** (I'm working) | I'm **not working** | **Am** I **working**? |
| He **is working** (He's working) | He **isn't working** | **Is** he **working**? |
| We **are working** (We're working) | We **aren't working** | **Are** we **working**? |

**Reglas de escritura de *-ing*:**

| Caso | Regla | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| Normal | + *ing* | work → work**ing**, check → check**ing** |
| Termina en *-e* muda | quita la *e* | writ**e** → wr**iting**, merg**e** → mer**ging**, releas**e** → releas**ing**, updat**e** → upda**ting** |
| 1 sílaba: consonante+vocal+consonante | dobla la consonante | run → ru**nn**ing, get → ge**tt**ing, plan → pla**nn**ing, stop → sto**pp**ing, ship → shi**pp**ing, set → se**tt**ing |
| Termina en *-ie* | *-ie* → *-y* | l**ie** → l**ying**, d**ie** → d**ying** |
| Termina en *-y* | no cambia | deploy → deploy**ing**, try → try**ing** |
| 2 sílabas con acento final | dobla | begin → begi**nn**ing, refer → refe**rr**ing, commit → commi**tt**ing, prefer → prefe**rr**ing |
| 2 sílabas con acento inicial | no dobla | **o**pen → open**ing**, **ha**ppen → happen**ing**, **of**fer → offer**ing** |

> Ojo: *commit* → *commit**t**ing* (acento en la segunda sílaba: com·**MIT**). *Benefit* → *benefiting* (acento inicial).

**Cuatro usos:**

| Uso | Ejemplo | Marcadores típicos |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Acción en este momento | *I'm reading the logs.* | now, right now, at the moment, as we speak |
| 2. Proyecto temporal en curso (no necesariamente ahora mismo) | *I'm working on the migration this sprint.* | this week, currently, these days |
| 3. Tendencia o cambio en progreso | *It's getting slower.* | more and more, increasingly |
| 4. Plan futuro acordado | *I'm meeting the client on Friday.* | tomorrow, next week |

### 3 · Notice — presente simple vs continuo

| Presente simple | Presente continuo |
|---|---|
| Permanente / rutina | Temporal / en curso |
| *I **work** on the payments team.* (mi puesto) | *I'm **working** on payments this sprint.* (asignación temporal) |
| *The service **runs** on port 8080.* (siempre) | *The job **is running** right now.* (en este instante) |
| *It **fails** every Friday.* (patrón) | *It**'s failing** right now.* (ahora) |
| *We **deploy** twice a week.* (frecuencia) | *We**'re deploying** at 4.* (hoy) |

**El contraste con consecuencias reales en el trabajo:**
- *"The tests fail."* → siempre fallan, están rotos.
- *"The tests are failing."* → están fallando ahora, quizá temporalmente.

Decir la forma incorrecta en un incidente cambia la interpretación del problema.

### 4 · Notice — verbos que no usan continuo (*state verbs*)

Algunos verbos describen **estados**, no acciones, y normalmente **no** van en *-ing*. Este es un error muy frecuente porque el español sí permite "estoy sabiendo", "lo estoy queriendo" en algunos contextos.

| Categoría | Verbos |
|---|---|
| Pensamiento | know, understand, think (=opinar), believe, remember, forget, mean, realize, doubt, suppose, agree |
| Emoción | want, need, like, love, hate, prefer, mind, care |
| Sentidos | see, hear, smell, taste, sound, seem, appear, look (=parecer) |
| Posesión | have (=poseer), own, belong, contain, consist, include, involve |
| Otros | cost, depend, matter, exist, fit, deserve |

| ❌ | ✅ |
|---|---|
| *I'm knowing the answer.* | I **know** the answer. |
| *I'm not understanding.* | I **don't understand**. |
| *I'm wanting more time.* | I **want** more time. |
| *This is depending on the config.* | This **depends** on the config. |
| *It's containing three fields.* | It **contains** three fields. |
| *I'm agreeing with you.* | I **agree** with you. |
| *I'm needing help.* | I **need** help. |

**Excepciones con cambio de significado:**

| Estado (simple) | Acción (continuo) |
|---|---|
| *I **think** it's a race condition.* (opino) | *I**'m thinking** about the design.* (proceso mental) |
| *I **have** two services.* (poseo) | *I**'m having** trouble with the build.* (experimento) |
| *It **looks** wrong.* (parece) | *I**'m looking** at the logs.* (miro) |
| *I **see** the problem.* (entiendo) | *I**'m seeing** timeouts.* (observo repetidamente) |

> *"I'm seeing errors in the logs"* es correcto y muy usado: significa observación repetida en curso. Es la excepción más útil de esta tabla.

### 5 · Sound

**Foco:** la terminación *-ing* → /ɪŋ/. Dos errores:
1. Pronunciar la /ɡ/ final: ❌ "wor-kin-**gue**". La /ŋ/ es un solo sonido nasal.
2. Usar /iː/ en lugar de /ɪ/: ❌ "wor-k**ee**n". Es /ɪ/ corta y relajada.

| Palabra | IPA |
|---|---|
| working | /ˈwɝːkɪŋ/ |
| running | /ˈrʌnɪŋ/ |
| debugging | /diːˈbʌɡɪŋ/ |
| deploying | /dɪˈplɔɪɪŋ/ |
| merging | /ˈmɝːdʒɪŋ/ |
| waiting | /ˈweɪtɪŋ/ (¡flap! "WEI-ring") |
| getting | /ˈɡetɪŋ/ ("GE-ring") |
| looking into | /ˈlʊkɪŋ ˈɪntuː/ |

En habla informal *-ing* se reduce a /ɪn/: *"What're you workin' on?"*. Reconócelo al oírlo; no lo imites al escribir.

### 6 · Chunks

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **What are you working on?** | pregunta estándar |
| **I'm working on…** | tu respuesta estándar |
| **I'm looking into it.** | lo estoy investigando |
| **I'm on it.** | me pongo con ello (muy usado) |
| **I'm still waiting for…** | bloqueo |
| **It's taking longer than expected.** | retraso |
| **I'm making progress.** / **Some progress.** | avance |
| **I'm stuck on…** | atascado |
| **as we speak** | mientras hablamos |
| **at the moment / right now / currently** | ahora |
| **It's getting worse / better.** | tendencia |
| **Nothing's moving until…** | bloqueo total |
| **I'm about to…** | estoy a punto de |
| **I'm halfway through it.** | voy por la mitad |
| **Bear with me.** | ten paciencia un momento |

### 7 · Practice

**Ejercicio 4A — Presente simple o continuo.**
1. I usually ____ (start) at 9, but today I ____ (start) at 8 because of the release.
2. The service ____ (run) on port 8080. Right now it ____ (not respond).
3. What ____ you ____ (work) on this sprint?
4. It ____ (depend) on the config.
5. Sorry, I ____ (not understand) the question.
6. We ____ (deploy) twice a week, but this week we ____ (deploy) three times.
7. I ____ (see) a lot of timeouts in the logs lately.
8. He ____ (know) Kubernetes very well.
9. The tests ____ (fail) right now — someone broke `main`.
10. Latency ____ (get) worse every week.

**Ejercicio 4B — Escribe la forma *-ing*.**
run · write · commit · deploy · merge · get · set · plan · begin · open · release · debug · ship · update · try · refactor

**Ejercicio 4C — Corrige.**
1. *I'm not agree with that approach.*
2. *I'm knowing the answer.*
3. *What you are working on?*
4. *He is work on the migration.*
5. *I'm having a question.*
6. *This is depending on the load.*
7. *We are deploy tomorrow.*
8. *I'm needing more context.*

**Ejercicio 4D — Traduce.**
1. Estoy investigando el problema del timeout.
2. Está tardando más de lo esperado.
3. ¿En qué estás trabajando ahora mismo?
4. No lo entiendo. ¿Puedes explicarlo otra vez?
5. Sigo esperando la aprobación.
6. Está empeorando.
7. Estoy atascado en un problema de configuración.
8. Trabajo en el equipo de pagos, pero este sprint estoy ayudando a plataforma.

### 8 · Challenge

**Reto 7.** Escribe tu actualización de estado real para hoy en 4 frases: qué estás haciendo, qué te bloquea, quién te está ayudando y qué es lo siguiente. Usa continuo al menos tres veces y un verbo de estado en simple.

**Reto 8.** Escribe la misma actualización dos veces: (a) para Slack, informal y breve; (b) para un 1:1 con tu manager, más completa. Nota cómo cambia el registro sin cambiar la gramática.

### 9 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto |
|---|---|
| *I'm not agree.* | I **don't agree** / I **disagree**. |
| *I am agree.* | **I agree.** |
| *What are you doing tomorrow?* → *I go to the office.* | I**'m going** to the office. |
| *He is working here since 2020.* | He **has been working** here since 2020. (U10) |
| *I'm having 3 meetings today.* | I **have** three meetings today. |
| *Actually I'm working on X.* | **Currently** I'm working on X. |
| *It's depending on you.* | It **depends** on you. |
| *I'm thinking that is wrong.* | I **think** it's wrong. |

```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```


## UNIT 5 — Asking for things without sounding rude

**Nivel:** A1.3 · **Escenario:** pides una revisión de código, acceso a un sistema, ayuda con un bug, o cinco minutos del tiempo de alguien.
**Meta comunicativa:** pedir, ofrecer y dar permiso con el nivel de cortesía adecuado.
**Gramática:** modales *can, could, would, may, will* · imperativo · *let's* · *would you mind + -ing*.

### 1 · Por qué esta unidad importa más de lo que parece

En español, la cortesía se transmite mucho con la entonación y con el diminutivo ("¿me echas una manito?"). En inglés se transmite con **estructura gramatical**. Si traduces literalmente *"Revisa mi PR"* → *"Review my PR"*, has dado una **orden**. En un equipo internacional eso se lee como brusquedad o incluso como falta de respeto, aunque tu intención fuera neutra.

Este es probablemente el punto donde más reputación pierden los hispanohablantes en equipos anglófonos, y se arregla con seis estructuras.

### 2 · Input

> **YOU:** Hey Tom, do you have a minute?
> **TOM:** Sure, what's up?
> **YOU:** Could you take a look at my PR when you get a chance? It's not urgent.
> **TOM:** Yeah, I can do it after the stand-up. Is it a big one?
> **YOU:** It's about 200 lines. Also — would you mind explaining how the retry logic works? I can't quite follow it.
> **TOM:** No problem. Do you want to jump on a quick call?
> **YOU:** That would be great, thanks. Can I share my screen?
> **TOM:** Go ahead. Oh, and can you do me a favour? Could you add a test for the null case before you merge?
> **YOU:** Sure, I'll do that. Thanks for the help.
> **TOM:** Anytime.

### 3 · Notice — la escala de cortesía

Todas estas frases significan "revisa mi PR". Están ordenadas de más directa a más cortés:

| Nivel | Estructura | Cuándo usarla |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Orden | **Review my PR.** | casi nunca; solo en instrucciones escritas de documentación |
| 2. Directo neutro | **Can you review my PR?** | equipo cercano, tarea rutinaria |
| 3. Cortés estándar | **Could you review my PR?** | **por defecto**: usa esto siempre que dudes |
| 4. Cortés + suavizador | **Could you review my PR when you get a chance?** | cuando no es urgente |
| 5. Muy cortés | **Would you mind reviewing my PR?** | favor real, alguien senior, alguien de otro equipo |
| 6. Máxima distancia | **I was wondering if you could review my PR.** | primer contacto, alguien muy ocupado |

> **Regla práctica:** *could* es tu opción por defecto. *Can* es aceptable pero más directo. Nunca uses imperativo con personas.

### 4 · Notice — los modales

Un **modal** es un verbo auxiliar que añade matiz (posibilidad, permiso, obligación). Tres reglas mecánicas:

1. **Nunca lleva *-s*** en tercera persona: ✅ *He can.* ❌ *He cans.*
2. **Le sigue infinitivo sin *to***: ✅ *I can help.* ❌ *I can to help.*
3. **Niega e interroga sin *do***: ✅ *Can you help?* ❌ *Do you can help?*

| Modal | Significado | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| **can** | capacidad, permiso, petición informal | *I can deploy it.* / *Can I merge?* |
| **can't** | imposibilidad, prohibición | *I can't reproduce it.* |
| **could** | petición cortés, posibilidad, capacidad pasada | *Could you help?* / *It could be a race condition.* |
| **would** | condicional, oferta, preferencia | *Would you like some help?* / *That would be great.* |
| **may** | permiso formal, posibilidad | *May I ask a question?* |
| **will** | futuro, promesa, voluntad | *I'll take a look.* |
| **shall** | propuesta (formal/UK) | *Shall we start?* |
| **should** | consejo, recomendación | *You should add a test.* |
| **must** | obligación fuerte | *You must not commit secrets.* |

### 5 · Notice — las seis estructuras que necesitas

**A) Pedir que alguien haga algo**

| Estructura | Nota |
|---|---|
| **Can/Could you** + infinitivo? | *Could you review this?* |
| **Would you mind** + verbo**-ing**? | ⚠️ *-ing*, no infinitivo: *Would you mind **taking** a look?* |
| **Do you think you could** + inf? | muy suave |
| **I was wondering if you could** + inf | máxima cortesía |
| **Any chance you could** + inf? | informal pero cortés |

> ⚠️ **Trampa de *would you mind*:** *mind* significa "importar/molestar". Por tanto la respuesta afirmativa es **negativa**: *"Would you mind helping?" — "**No**, not at all."* = "no me importa, claro que ayudo". Si respondes *"Yes"*, estás diciendo que **sí** te molesta.

**B) Pedir permiso**

| Estructura | Formalidad |
|---|---|
| **Can I** + inf? | informal |
| **Could I** + inf? | estándar |
| **May I** + inf? | formal |
| **Is it OK if I** + presente? | muy natural: *Is it OK if I merge this now?* |
| **Do you mind if I** + presente? | *Do you mind if I take this ticket?* |
| **Would it be OK to** + inf? | |

**C) Ofrecer ayuda**

| Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| **Do you want me to** + inf? | *Do you want me to take a look?* |
| **Shall I** + inf? | *Shall I roll it back?* (UK) |
| **Would you like me to** + inf? | formal |
| **I can** + inf **if you want.** | *I can pair with you if you want.* |
| **Let me** + inf. | *Let me check the logs.* |
| **Do you need a hand with that?** | informal |

**D) Proponer algo conjunto**

| Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| **Let's** + inf | *Let's take this offline.* |
| **Why don't we** + inf? | *Why don't we split the PR?* |
| **How about** + verbo-ing? | *How about splitting it?* |
| **Shall we** + inf? | *Shall we jump on a call?* |
| **What if we** + presente? | *What if we cache the response?* |
| **Maybe we should** + inf | *Maybe we should revert first.* |

**E) Aceptar y rechazar**

| Aceptar | Rechazar (con cortesía) |
|---|---|
| Sure. / Of course. | I'm afraid I can't right now. |
| No problem. | Sorry, I'm a bit swamped today. |
| Absolutely. | I'd rather not, to be honest. |
| Yeah, go ahead. | Can it wait until tomorrow? |
| Happy to. | I don't think I'm the right person — try Maya. |
| Consider it done. | I'd love to, but I'm on call this week. |

**F) Agradecer y responder al agradecimiento**

| Agradecer | Responder |
|---|---|
| Thanks. / Thank you. | No problem. |
| Thanks a lot. | You're welcome. |
| Thanks for the help. | Anytime. |
| I appreciate it. | Sure thing. |
| That's really helpful, thanks. | Happy to help. |
| Thanks for the quick turnaround. | No worries. |

### 6 · Sound

**Foco:** los modales en habla real se **reducen** brutalmente. Reconocerlos es la mitad de tu problema de comprensión.

| Escrito | Habla real | IPA |
|---|---|---|
| Could you… | "cuh-juh" | /ˈkʊdʒə/ |
| Would you… | "wuh-juh" | /ˈwʊdʒə/ |
| Can you… | "k'n yuh" | /kən jə/ |
| Do you want to… | "d'ya wanna" | /djə ˈwɑːnə/ |
| I will → **I'll** | "ail" | /aɪl/ |
| I would → **I'd** | "aid" | /aɪd/ |
| can't | | /kænt/ US · /kɑːnt/ UK |
| Let me → **lemme** | | /ˈlemi/ |
| Shall we | | /ˈʃæl wi/ |

> **Diferencia clave *can* vs *can't*:** en habla rápida americana la /t/ de *can't* casi desaparece. Lo que distingue las dos es que **can** se reduce a /kən/ (débil, átona) y **can't** se pronuncia /kænt/ (fuerte, tónica, vocal clara /æ/). Si oyes la vocal clara, es negativo.
> *"I can DO it"* /aɪ kən ˈduː ɪt/ vs *"I CAN'T do it"* /aɪ ˈkænt duː ɪt/.

### 7 · Chunks

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **Do you have a minute?** | abrir una petición |
| **What's up?** | respuesta informal |
| **when you get a chance** | quita urgencia |
| **no rush** / **no pressure** | idem |
| **Could you do me a favour?** [UK] / **favor** [US] | |
| **Would you mind taking a look?** | |
| **Do you want to jump on a quick call?** | |
| **Can I share my screen?** | |
| **Let's take this offline.** | seguir fuera de la reunión |
| **I'm a bit swamped.** | estoy desbordado |
| **I'll get to it this afternoon.** | me pondré con ello |
| **Consider it done.** | dalo por hecho |
| **Feel free to…** | siéntete libre de |
| **Let me know if…** | avísame si |
| **Sorry to bother you, but…** | perdona que te moleste |
| **Thanks for the quick turnaround.** | gracias por la rapidez |

### 8 · Practice

**Ejercicio 5A — Reescribe estas órdenes como peticiones corteses.** Usa una estructura distinta en cada una.
1. *Review my PR.*
2. *Explain the retry logic.*
3. *Send me the logs.*
4. *Give me access to the staging database.*
5. *Don't merge yet.*
6. *Help me with this bug.*

**Ejercicio 5B — Elige la respuesta correcta.**
1. *"Would you mind reviewing this?"* — Quieres decir que sí ayudas.
   a) *Yes, I would.* b) *No, not at all.* c) *Yes, of course.*
2. *"Do you mind if I merge this?"* — Quieres decir que no hay problema.
   a) *No, go ahead.* b) *Yes, go ahead.*
3. *"Can I get access?"* — Se lo deniegas con cortesía.
   a) *No.* b) *I'm afraid you'll need approval from Priya first.*

**Ejercicio 5C — Completa con el modal adecuado.**
1. ____ you take a look at this when you get a chance?
2. I ____ reproduce it locally — it only happens in staging.
3. ____ I ask a quick question? *(formal)*
4. That ____ be great, thanks.
5. You ____ commit secrets to the repo. *(prohibición fuerte)*
6. You ____ add a test for that edge case. *(consejo)*
7. It ____ be a caching issue, but I'm not sure. *(posibilidad)*
8. ____ we split this into two PRs? *(propuesta)*

**Ejercicio 5D — Corrige.**
1. *Can you to review my PR?*
2. *He cans help you.*
3. *Do you can help me?*
4. *Would you mind to explain this?*
5. *I'm afraid that I can't to do it today.*
6. *Please, review my code.* *(no incorrecto, pero mejorable)*
7. *Let's to start.*
8. *Shall I to roll it back?*

**Ejercicio 5E — Repaso intercalado (U1–U4).** Traduce.
1. Hay dos tests que fallan ahora mismo.
2. No estoy de acuerdo, pero puedo estar equivocado.
3. ¿Cuántos servicios tienes en producción?
4. Normalmente no despliego los viernes.

### 9 · Challenge

**Reto 9.** Escribe tres mensajes de Slack reales: (a) pedir a un compañero cercano que revise un PR pequeño; (b) pedir a un ingeniero senior de otro equipo que te explique un sistema que no entiendes; (c) rechazar con cortesía una petición porque estás desbordado. Cada uno debe usar una estructura de cortesía distinta.

**Reto 10.** Coge los tres últimos mensajes que hayas escrito en inglés en el trabajo. Reescríbelos subiendo un nivel en la escala de cortesía. Compara.

### 10 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| *Please review my PR.* | Could you review my PR? | *please* + imperativo sigue siendo orden |
| *Can you to help?* | Can you **help**? | modal + infinitivo sin *to* |
| *Would you mind to explain?* | Would you mind **explaining**? | *mind* + *-ing* |
| *He can helps.* | He can **help**. | |
| *I hope you can help me.* (en frío) | I'd really appreciate your help with… | traducción literal suena a súplica |
| *I need that you review this.* | I need **you to review** this. | |
| *It's necessary that you add a test.* | You **should** add a test. | el español legaliza; el inglés aconseja |
| *Tell me if you have doubts.* | Let me know if you have **any questions**. | *doubt* = duda escéptica, no pregunta |
| *I wait your response.* | I look forward to hearing from you. / Let me know. | |
| *Excuse me, can I make a question?* | Can I **ask** a question? | *ask*, no *make* |

## UNIT 6 — What did you do yesterday?

**Nivel:** A1.4 · **Escenario:** el stand-up diario. La primera pregunta es siempre sobre ayer.
**Meta comunicativa:** narrar acciones terminadas en el pasado.
**Gramática:** pasado simple regular e irregular · *did / didn't* · *was / were* · expresiones de tiempo pasado.

### 1 · Input

> **PRIYA:** Let's start. Andrés, what did you do yesterday?
> **YOU:** Yesterday I finished the retry logic and I opened a PR. Tom reviewed it in the afternoon and I fixed the two comments he left. Then I ran the load test, but it failed — the p99 went up to four seconds.
> **PRIYA:** Did you find out why?
> **YOU:** Not yet. I didn't have time to dig into it. I checked the logs but I didn't see anything obvious.
> **PRIYA:** Was the database under load at the time?
> **YOU:** I don't know. That's a good point — I didn't look at the DB metrics.
> **PRIYA:** OK. And what about Tom? Were you two pairing?
> **TOM:** We were, for about an hour. Then I got pulled into the incident. Someone deleted a production index by mistake.
> **PRIYA:** Right, I saw that. Did we recover?
> **TOM:** We did, but it took two hours. I wrote a postmortem draft last night.

### 2 · Notice — pasado simple regular

**Afirmativo:** verbo + **-ed**. Igual para todas las personas — sin excepciones, sin tercera persona especial.

| Sujeto | Verbo |
|---|---|
| I / you / he / she / it / we / they | **deployed**, **fixed**, **reviewed** |

**Reglas de escritura:**

| Caso | Regla | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| Normal | + *ed* | check → check**ed**, deploy → deploy**ed** |
| Termina en *-e* | + *d* | merg**e** → merg**ed**, releas**e** → releas**ed** |
| Consonante + *-y* | *-y* → *-ied* | tr**y** → tr**ied**, appl**y** → appl**ied**, retr**y** → retr**ied** |
| Vocal + *-y* | + *ed* | deploy → deploy**ed**, play → play**ed** |
| 1 sílaba C-V-C | dobla | stop → sto**pp**ed, plan → pla**nn**ed, ship → shi**pp**ed, drop → dro**pp**ed |
| 2 sílabas con acento final | dobla | commit → commi**tt**ed, refer → refe**rr**ed, prefer → prefe**rr**ed |
| 2 sílabas con acento inicial | no dobla | **o**pen → opened, **hap**pen → happened, **tar**get → targeted |

### 3 · Notice — los irregulares que usarás cada día

Los verbos irregulares no siguen la regla *-ed*. Hay unos 180; estos 45 cubren el 95% de tu inglés profesional. **Memoriza las tres columnas juntas**, aunque el participio no lo necesites hasta la Unidad 10.

| Infinitivo | Pasado | Participio | Español |
|---|---|---|---|
| be | was / were | been | ser/estar |
| have | had | had | tener |
| do | did | done | hacer |
| go | went | gone | ir |
| get | got | got/gotten | obtener |
| make | made | made | hacer/crear |
| take | took | taken | tomar |
| see | saw | seen | ver |
| come | came | come | venir |
| find | found | found | encontrar |
| think | thought | thought | pensar |
| know | knew | known | saber |
| write | wrote | written | escribir |
| run | ran | run | ejecutar |
| build | built | built | compilar |
| break | broke | broken | romper |
| send | sent | sent | enviar |
| read | read /red/ | read /red/ | leer |
| tell | told | told | decir a |
| say | said /sed/ | said | decir |
| give | gave | given | dar |
| leave | left | left | dejar/irse |
| put | put | put | poner |
| set | set | set | establecer |
| cut | cut | cut | cortar |
| let | let | let | permitir |
| hit | hit | hit | golpear |
| spend | spent | spent | gastar/pasar (tiempo) |
| lose | lost | lost | perder |
| keep | kept | kept | mantener |
| mean | meant /ment/ | meant | significar |
| feel | felt | felt | sentir |
| understand | understood | understood | entender |
| catch | caught | caught | capturar |
| bring | brought | brought | traer |
| buy | bought | bought | comprar |
| choose | chose | chosen | elegir |
| forget | forgot | forgotten | olvidar |
| begin | began | begun | empezar |
| speak | spoke | spoken | hablar |
| hold | held | held | sostener/mantener |
| stand | stood | stood | estar de pie |
| throw | threw | thrown | lanzar |
| rise | rose | risen | subir (intr.) |
| fall | fell | fallen | caer |
| grow | grew | grown | crecer |

> Lista ampliada en el **Apéndice A**.

### 4 · Notice — negativo e interrogativo: *did*

Aquí el inglés simplifica: en negativo e interrogativo se usa **did + infinitivo**, y el verbo principal **pierde toda marca de pasado**, sea regular o irregular.

| Afirmativo | Negativo | Interrogativo |
|---|---|---|
| I **deployed** it. | I **didn't deploy** it. | **Did** you **deploy** it? |
| He **wrote** the test. | He **didn't write** the test. | **Did** he **write** the test? |
| It **broke**. | It **didn't break**. | **Did** it **break**? |
| We **ran** it. | We **didn't run** it. | **Did** we **run** it? |

> ❌ *Did you deployed it?* ❌ *I didn't wrote it.* ❌ *Did he went?*
> **Regla:** el pasado solo se marca **una vez**. Si está en *did*, no está en el verbo.

**Respuestas cortas:** *Did it work? — Yes, it **did**. / No, it **didn't**.*

**Excepción: *was / were*** no usa *did*.

| | Afirmativo | Negativo | Interrogativo |
|---|---|---|---|
| I / he / she / it | **was** | **wasn't** | **Was** it…? |
| you / we / they | **were** | **weren't** | **Were** you…? |

*Was the database under load?* · *The tests weren't green.* · *Were you pairing?*

**Excepción: *who* como sujeto** no lleva *did*: *Who **deleted** the index?* (no *Who did delete*, salvo énfasis).

### 5 · Expresiones de tiempo pasado

| Expresión | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| yesterday | *I deployed it yesterday.* |
| last night / week / sprint / month | *last sprint* (sin *the*) |
| **X ago** | *two hours ago*, *a week ago* (¡*ago* va **después**!) |
| this morning | (si ya pasó) |
| the day before yesterday | anteayer |
| on Monday | el lunes (pasado) |
| in 2023 | |
| at the time | en ese momento |
| earlier today | antes hoy |
| just now | ahora mismo (acabado) |
| back then | por entonces |
| when I joined | cuando entré |

> ❌ *ago two hours* ✅ *two hours **ago***
> ❌ *the last week* ✅ *last week*

### 6 · Sound

**Foco:** la triple pronunciación de *-ed* (sección 1.5) aplicada al pasado. Es lo que te delata como principiante.

| Verbo | -ed | IPA |
|---|---|---|
| finish**ed** | /t/ | /ˈfɪnɪʃt/ |
| fix**ed** | /t/ | /fɪkst/ |
| push**ed** | /t/ | /pʊʃt/ |
| check**ed** | /t/ | /tʃekt/ |
| stop**ped** | /t/ | /stɑːpt/ |
| review**ed** | /d/ | /rɪˈvjuːd/ |
| deploy**ed** | /d/ | /dɪˈplɔɪd/ |
| merg**ed** | /d/ | /mɝːdʒd/ |
| fail**ed** | /d/ | /feɪld/ |
| clon**ed** | /d/ | /kloʊnd/ |
| upda**ted** | /ɪd/ | /ˈʌpdeɪtɪd/ |
| test**ed** | /ɪd/ | /ˈtestɪd/ |
| delet**ed** | /ɪd/ | /dɪˈliːtɪd/ |
| start**ed** | /ɪd/ | /ˈstɑːrtɪd/ |
| need**ed** | /ɪd/ | /ˈniːdɪd/ |

**Irregulares con trampa fonética:**
- *read* (pasado) se escribe igual pero suena **/red/**, no /riːd/.
- *said* suena **/sed/**, no /seɪd/.
- *meant* suena **/ment/**.
- *caught, brought, thought, bought* riman todos: /ɔːt/.

### 7 · Chunks

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **Yesterday I finished…** | apertura de stand-up |
| **I picked up the ticket for…** | cogí la tarea de |
| **I opened a PR for…** | |
| **Tom left a couple of comments.** | |
| **I addressed the comments.** | resolví los comentarios |
| **It failed / It passed.** | |
| **I didn't have time to…** | |
| **I didn't get to it.** | no llegué a hacerlo |
| **I got pulled into an incident.** | me metieron en un incidente |
| **It took about two hours.** | |
| **I dug into the logs.** | *dig → dug* |
| **I couldn't reproduce it.** | |
| **I ended up rewriting it.** | acabé reescribiéndolo |
| **It turned out to be a config issue.** | resultó ser |
| **That's a good point.** | reconocer una observación |
| **by mistake / by accident** | sin querer |

### 8 · Practice

**Ejercicio 6A — Escribe el pasado.**
finish · fix · try · deploy · stop · commit · merge · plan · open · happen · run · write · build · break · send · read · think · get · make · take · see · find · leave · put · lose · spend · catch · choose · begin · understand

**Ejercicio 6B — Completa en pasado simple.**
1. Yesterday I ____ (finish) the retry logic and ____ (open) a PR.
2. Tom ____ (review) it, and I ____ (fix) his comments.
3. The load test ____ (fail) — the p99 ____ (go) up to four seconds.
4. I ____ (not have) time to investigate.
5. ____ you ____ (find) out why?
6. Someone ____ (delete) a production index by mistake.
7. It ____ (take) two hours to recover.
8. I ____ (write) the postmortem last night.
9. The tests ____ (not pass) in CI, but they ____ (work) locally.
10. ____ the database ____ (be) under load at the time?

**Ejercicio 6C — Corrige.**
1. *Did you deployed it?*
2. *I didn't wrote the test.*
3. *He no came to the stand-up.*
4. *Yesterday I have finished the PR.*
5. *Ago two hours it failed.*
6. *The tests was green.*
7. *Who did delete the index?*
8. *I was reviewed the PR.*
9. *It taked two hours.*
10. *Last the week we deployed twice.*

**Ejercicio 6D — Convierte a negativo y pregunta.**
1. He merged the PR.
2. It broke the build.
3. They found the root cause.
4. The pipeline was green.
5. We ran the migration.

**Ejercicio 6E — Traduce.**
1. Ayer terminé la lógica de reintentos y abrí un PR.
2. No tuve tiempo de investigarlo.
3. ¿Encontraste la causa raíz?
4. Alguien borró un índice de producción sin querer.
5. Tardó dos horas en recuperarse.
6. Los tests no pasaron en CI, pero funcionaban en local.
7. Resultó ser un problema de configuración.
8. Hace dos horas el servicio estaba caído.

### 9 · Challenge

**Reto 11.** Escribe tu stand-up real de ayer en 5–7 frases, todo en pasado simple. Requisitos: tres verbos irregulares, una negación con *didn't*, y una expresión de tiempo con *ago*.

**Reto 12 (oral, 45 segundos).** Grábate diciéndolo. Después escucha **solo** las terminaciones *-ed*: comprueba que distingues /t/, /d/ e /ɪd/. Es el ejercicio de práctica deliberada más rentable de todo el nivel A1.

**Reto 13.** Escribe un mini-postmortem de un incidente real que hayas vivido, en 8 frases y solo con pasado simple: qué pasó, cuándo, qué hicisteis, cuánto tardó, qué aprendisteis.

### 10 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| *Did you finished?* | Did you **finish**? | pasado solo una vez |
| *I didn't saw it.* | I didn't **see** it. | |
| *Yesterday I have deployed.* | Yesterday I **deployed**. | con tiempo pasado explícito → pasado simple |
| *It taked / It costed* | It **took** / It **cost** | |
| *The team were / was?* | ambos válidos: *the team was* [US] / *were* [UK] | |
| *I was working there 3 years.* | I **worked** there **for** 3 years. | |
| *When I have arrived, it was broken.* | When I **arrived**, it was broken. | |
| *He said me that…* | He **told me** that… / He **said** that… | *say* no lleva objeto de persona directo |
| *I explained him the bug.* | I **explained the bug to him**. | |
| *We discussed about it.* | We **discussed it**. | *discuss* no lleva preposición |
