# PARTE 3 — NIVEL A2

> **Objetivo:** al terminar la Unidad 12 serás autónomo en un stand-up, podrás comparar opciones técnicas, dar una estimación, hablar de tu experiencia profesional, escribir instrucciones y explicar un bug. Esto es el umbral de la empleabilidad internacional real.

## UNIT 7 — The complete stand-up

**Nivel:** A2.1 · **Escenario:** el stand-up diario completo, con las tres partes: ayer, hoy, bloqueos.
**Meta comunicativa:** integrar pasado, presente e intención futura en 30 segundos.
**Gramática:** *going to* · *be + to* · integración de los tres tiempos · lenguaje de bloqueos.

### 1 · Input

> **PRIYA:** OK, quick round. Maya, you first.
> **MAYA:** Yesterday I wrapped up the schema migration and got it merged. Today I'm going to start on the backfill script — I'm aiming to have a draft by end of day. No blockers.
> **TOM:** Yesterday I spent most of the day on the incident. Today I'm finishing the postmortem and then I'm picking up the flaky test ticket. I'm blocked on one thing: I need access to the production dashboards. Priya, could you sort that out?
> **PRIYA:** I'll ping the platform team after this.
> **YOU:** Yesterday I looked into the timeout issue but I didn't get to the bottom of it. Today I'm going to reproduce it in staging with extra logging. If that doesn't work, I'll try running a load test locally. I'm not blocked, but I might need Tom for half an hour later.
> **TOM:** Sure, just ping me.
> **PRIYA:** Great. Anything else? No? Let's wrap up.

### 2 · Notice — la anatomía del stand-up

Un stand-up es una estructura fija de tres bloques. Aprende la estructura y no tendrás que improvisar nunca.

| Bloque | Tiempo verbal | Plantilla |
|---|---|---|
| **1. Ayer** | pasado simple | *Yesterday I* + pasado |
| **2. Hoy** | presente continuo o *going to* | *Today I'm* + -ing / *I'm going to* + inf |
| **3. Bloqueos** | presente | *I'm blocked on…* / *No blockers.* |

**Bloque 1 — Ayer (verbos de cierre y de progreso):**

| Chunk | Significado |
|---|---|
| I **wrapped up** the migration. | terminé del todo |
| I **finished** / **completed** X. | |
| I **got X merged**. | conseguí que se fusionara |
| I **made progress on** X. | avancé en |
| I **made a start on** X. | empecé |
| I **spent most of the day on** X. | pasé casi todo el día en |
| I **looked into** X. | investigué |
| I **didn't get to** X. | no llegué a X |
| I **didn't get to the bottom of it.** | no llegué al fondo del asunto |
| I **parked** X. | lo dejé aparcado |
| I **carried on with** X. | seguí con |

**Bloque 2 — Hoy:**

| Chunk | Matiz |
|---|---|
| Today I'm **working on** X. | en curso |
| Today I'm **going to start on** X. | intención decidida |
| I'm **picking up** the X ticket. | cogiendo la tarea |
| I'm **aiming to** have a draft by EOD. | objetivo |
| I'm **hoping to** finish it today. | esperanza, menos firme |
| I'll **probably** get to X. | probabilidad |
| I'm **planning to** pair with Maya. | plan |
| First I'm going to X, **then** I'll Y. | secuencia |

**Bloque 3 — Bloqueos (el bloque que más importa):**

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **No blockers.** | todo bien |
| **Nothing blocking.** | idem |
| **I'm blocked on X.** | bloqueo duro |
| **I'm waiting on X.** / **waiting for approval.** | espera |
| **I need access to X.** | |
| **I could use a hand with X.** | pedir ayuda sin dramatizar |
| **I might need X later.** | aviso preventivo |
| **This is a soft blocker** — I can work around it for now. | bloqueo parcial |
| **It's not blocking me yet, but it will be by Thursday.** | anticipar |
| **Can we take this offline?** | discutir después |

> **Diferencia clave:** *waiting **for** someone* (persona/cosa) vs *waiting **on** something* (dependencia, uso corporativo muy común). Ambas se oyen; *waiting on the DBA* y *waiting for the DBA* son igualmente aceptables en contexto profesional.

### 3 · Notice — *going to*

**Estructura:** *am/is/are* + **going to** + infinitivo.

| | |
|---|---|
| Afirmativo | *I'm going to refactor it.* |
| Negativo | *I'm not going to refactor it.* |
| Interrogativo | *Are you going to refactor it?* |

**Dos usos:**

1. **Intención decidida antes de hablar** (lo más frecuente en stand-ups):
   *I'm going to start on the backfill today.* → ya lo había decidido.
2. **Predicción basada en evidencia visible**:
   *Look at the queue — it's going to overflow.* → lo veo venir por la evidencia.

**Contraste con *will* (adelanto de la Unidad 9):**

| *going to* | *will* |
|---|---|
| decisión previa | decisión **en el momento** |
| *I'm going to review it after lunch.* (ya lo tenía planeado) | *OK, I'**ll** review it now.* (lo decido al oírte) |
| evidencia presente | predicción/opinión |
| *It's going to fail.* (veo la métrica) | *I think it'**ll** fail.* (creo) |

**En habla real:** *going to* se reduce a **gonna** /ˈɡʌnə/. *"I'm gonna check the logs."* Reconócelo siempre; **no lo escribas** en comunicación profesional.

### 4 · Notice — presente continuo con valor de futuro

Cuando algo está **acordado y agendado**, el inglés usa presente continuo, no *will*:

- *I'**m meeting** the client on Thursday.* (está en el calendario)
- *We'**re deploying** at 4 p.m.*
- *He'**s joining** the team next month.*
- *I'**m taking** Friday off.*

| Estructura | Grado de compromiso |
|---|---|
| *I'm deploying at 4.* | agendado, confirmado |
| *I'm going to deploy at 4.* | lo he decidido |
| *I'll deploy at 4.* | lo decido ahora / me ofrezco |
| *I might deploy at 4.* | posible |

### 5 · Sound

**Foco:** reducciones de futuro. Sin esto no entenderás un stand-up de nativos.

| Escrito | Habla real | IPA |
|---|---|---|
| going to | gonna | /ˈɡʌnə/ |
| want to | wanna | /ˈwɑːnə/ |
| I will | I'll | /aɪl/ |
| we will | we'll | /wɪl/ o /wiːl/ |
| he will | he'll | /hiːl/ |
| it will | it'll | /ˈɪtəl/ ("I-rl") |
| they will | they'll | /ðeɪl/ |
| that will | that'll | /ˈðætəl/ |
| I am going to | "I'm-gonna" | /aɪmˈɡʌnə/ |
| got to | gotta | /ˈɡɑːtə/ |
| out of | outta | /ˈaʊtə/ |
| kind of | kinda | /ˈkaɪndə/ |
| a lot of | "a lotta" | /əˈlɑːtə/ |

**Frase de entrenamiento:** *"I'm gonna pick it up after the stand-up, and if it's still failing I'll probably have to roll it back."*
/aɪm ˈɡʌnə ˈpɪkɪdʌp ˈæftər ðə ˈstændʌp, ənd ɪf ɪts ˈstɪl ˈfeɪlɪŋ aɪl ˈprɑːbli ˈhæftə ˈroʊlɪt ˈbæk/

### 6 · Practice

**Ejercicio 7A — *going to*, presente continuo o *will*.**
1. Today I ____ (start) on the migration. *(intención decidida)*
2. — The build is red. — OK, I ____ (take) a look right now. *(decisión inmediata)*
3. We ____ (deploy) at 4 p.m. It's on the calendar.
4. Look at the memory graph — it ____ (crash).
5. I think it ____ (be) a config issue, but I'm not sure.
6. I ____ (meet) the security team tomorrow at ten.
7. If the test fails again, I ____ (revert) it.

**Ejercicio 7B — Ordena este stand-up desordenado.**
a) *No blockers, but I might need Maya for a review.*
b) *Today I'm going to add the integration tests.*
c) *Yesterday I finished the parser and opened a PR.*
d) *I also spent an hour on the flaky test but I didn't get to the bottom of it.*

**Ejercicio 7C — Traduce.**
1. Ayer terminé la migración y conseguí que se fusionara.
2. Hoy voy a empezar con el script de backfill.
3. Mi objetivo es tener un borrador para el final del día.
4. Estoy bloqueado: necesito acceso a los dashboards de producción.
5. No estoy bloqueado, pero puede que necesite a Tom media hora más tarde.
6. Si eso no funciona, probaré con un test de carga en local.
7. Pasé casi todo el día en el incidente.
8. ¿Podemos hablarlo después de la reunión?

### 7 · Challenge

**Reto 14.** Escribe tu stand-up completo de hoy, con los tres bloques, en 6 frases. Cronométralo al leerlo: debe durar entre 25 y 40 segundos. Si dura más de un minuto, estás dando demasiado detalle — el stand-up es un titular, no un informe.

**Reto 15 (una semana).** Escribe tu stand-up cada día durante cinco días laborables. El viernes compáralos: verás que estás reutilizando 15 chunks. Ese es exactamente el objetivo: automatización.

```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```


## UNIT 8 — Comparing technologies and options

**Nivel:** A2.2 · **Escenario:** una reunión de decisión técnica: Postgres o MongoDB, REST o gRPC, monolito o microservicios.
**Meta comunicativa:** comparar, evaluar y justificar una preferencia.
**Gramática:** comparativos · superlativos · *as…as* · *than* · adjetivos graduables · lenguaje de trade-offs.

### 1 · Input

> **PRIYA:** So, Postgres or DynamoDB for the events table?
> **MAYA:** Postgres is simpler to operate — we already know it. DynamoDB is faster at this access pattern, but it's a lot harder to query ad hoc.
> **TOM:** It's also more expensive at our volume, isn't it?
> **MAYA:** Slightly. But the bigger issue is that it's much less flexible. Every new query means a new index.
> **YOU:** How much faster is DynamoDB, though? Are we talking twice as fast, or ten times?
> **MAYA:** At p99, maybe three times. But Postgres is fast enough for what we need — that's the point.
> **PRIYA:** So the trade-off is operational simplicity versus raw latency.
> **YOU:** Right. And in my experience, the least risky option is usually the one the team already knows. I'd rather have a slightly slower system we can debug than the fastest one nobody understands.
> **PRIYA:** That's a fair point. Let's go with Postgres for now and revisit it if latency becomes a problem.

### 2 · Notice — comparativos

**La regla depende del número de sílabas del adjetivo.**

| Tipo de adjetivo | Comparativo | Superlativo |
|---|---|---|
| **1 sílaba** | + *-er* | *the* + *-est* |
| *fast* | fast**er** | **the** fast**est** |
| *slow, cheap, hard, safe, clean, small, large* | slow**er**, cheap**er**… | |
| **1 sílaba, C-V-C** (dobla) | *big → big**ger*** | the big**gest** |
| *thin, hot, fit* | thi**nn**er | |
| **2 sílabas en *-y*** | *-y* → *-ier* | *-iest* |
| *easy, noisy, flaky, risky, dirty, tidy, busy* | eas**ier**, flak**ier**, risk**ier** | the eas**iest** |
| **2 sílabas (otras)** | ***more*** + adj | ***the most*** + adj |
| *complex, stable, robust, common, useful, verbose* | **more** complex | **the most** complex |
| **3+ sílabas** | ***more*** + adj | ***the most*** + adj |
| *expensive, maintainable, performant, reliable, scalable* | **more** expensive | **the most** expensive |

**Irregulares:**

| Adjetivo | Comparativo | Superlativo |
|---|---|---|
| good | **better** | the **best** |
| bad | **worse** | the **worst** |
| far | further / farther | the furthest |
| little | less | the least |
| much / many | more | the most |

> ❌ *more better* ❌ *more easier* ❌ *the more fast* — nunca combines *more* con *-er*.

**Estructura de comparación:** adjetivo comparativo + **than**.
*Postgres is simpler **than** DynamoDB.* · *This approach is **more** maintainable **than** the current one.*

> ❌ *simpler that* ❌ *simpler as* ✅ *simpler **than***

### 3 · Notice — grados de diferencia (aquí está el matiz profesional)

Decir *"it's faster"* es pobre. Un ingeniero cuantifica.

| Intensidad | Modificador | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| Diferencia enorme | **far / much / a lot / significantly / considerably** | *far more reliable*, *much slower*, *significantly cheaper* |
| Diferencia notable | **quite a bit / noticeably** | *quite a bit faster* |
| Diferencia pequeña | **slightly / a bit / a little / marginally** | *slightly more expensive* |
| Sin diferencia | **just as / equally / no + comparativo** | *just as fast*, *no better* |
| Con cifra | número + comparativo | *three times faster*, *20% cheaper*, *two orders of magnitude slower* |

> ⚠️ **Trampa:** con comparativos usa **much/far**, nunca *very*. ❌ *very faster* ✅ *much faster*.

### 4 · Notice — *as … as*

| Estructura | Significado |
|---|---|
| **as** + adj + **as** | igual de… que: *It's **as fast as** Redis.* |
| **not as** + adj + **as** | no tan… como: *It's **not as flexible as** SQL.* |
| **twice / three times as** + adj + **as** | dos/tres veces más… que: *It's **twice as expensive as** the alternative.* |
| **the same as** | igual que: *The latency is **the same as** before.* |
| **similar to** | parecido a: *It's **similar to** Kafka.* |
| **different from** | distinto de: *It's **different from** what we use.* |
| **as** + adj + **as possible** | lo más… posible: *Keep it **as simple as possible**.* |
| adj + **enough** | suficientemente: *It's **fast enough**.* |
| **too** + adj | demasiado: *It's **too risky**.* |

> ⚠️ *enough* va **después** del adjetivo (*fast enough*) pero **antes** del sustantivo (*enough time*).
> ⚠️ ❌ *different to/than* (aceptado en UK/US informal, pero usa *different **from*** para escribir bien).

### 5 · Notice — superlativos

**Estructura:** *the* + superlativo (+ *of/in* + grupo).

- *the fastest option* · *the most reliable service* · *the least risky approach*
- *the biggest problem **in** the system* (lugar) · *the best **of** the three* (grupo)
- *one of the most common mistakes* (⚠️ *one of the* + superlativo + **plural**)

### 6 · Vocabulario: adjetivos de evaluación técnica

| Adjetivo | IPA | Significado |
|---|---|---|
| fast / slow | | |
| simple / complex | /ˈkɑːmpleks/ | |
| reliable | /rɪˈlaɪəbəl/ | fiable |
| maintainable | /meɪnˈteɪnəbəl/ | mantenible |
| scalable | /ˈskeɪləbəl/ | escalable |
| robust | /roʊˈbʌst/ | robusto |
| brittle / fragile | /ˈbrɪtəl/ | frágil, quebradizo |
| flaky | /ˈfleɪki/ | inestable (tests) |
| verbose | /vərˈboʊs/ | verboso |
| lightweight / heavyweight | | ligero / pesado |
| opinionated | /əˈpɪnjəneɪtɪd/ | con criterios impuestos |
| battle-tested | | probado en producción |
| bleeding edge | | tecnología muy nueva y arriesgada |
| mature | /məˈtʃʊr/ | maduro |
| overkill | | excesivo para el caso |
| straightforward | /ˌstreɪtˈfɔːrwərd/ | sencillo, directo |
| tricky | | delicado, complicado |
| expensive / costly | | |
| performant | /pərˈfɔːrmənt/ | de buen rendimiento |
| coupled / decoupled | /ˈkʌpəld/ | acoplado / desacoplado |
| readable | | legible |
| error-prone | | propenso a errores |

### 7 · Lenguaje de trade-offs (B1 anticipado, muy rentable)

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **The trade-off is X versus Y.** | plantear el compromiso |
| **It's a trade-off between speed and simplicity.** | |
| **On the one hand… on the other hand…** | dos caras |
| **The downside is…** / **The upside is…** | pro y contra |
| **The main advantage is…** / **drawback is…** | |
| **It comes at the cost of…** | tiene el coste de |
| **X is fine for now, but it won't scale.** | |
| **It depends on the use case.** | |
| **In my experience…** | anclar en autoridad propia |
| **I'd rather have X than Y.** | preferencia |
| **I'd lean towards X.** | me inclinaría por |
| **That's a fair point.** | conceder |
| **Let's revisit this if X becomes a problem.** | aparcar con criterio |
| **It's not a blocker either way.** | |

> **Nota sobre *I'd rather*:** va con infinitivo **sin *to***: *I'd rather **use** Postgres.* Y para comparar: *I'd rather use Postgres **than** Mongo.*

### 8 · Sound

**Foco:** *-er* final y *than*, ambos con **schwa** /ər/ y /ðən/. Un hispanohablante los pronuncia con vocal plena y suena entrecortado.

| Escrito | IPA real |
|---|---|
| faster than | /ˈfæstər ðən/ → "FAS-ter-then" enlazado |
| simpler than | /ˈsɪmplər ðən/ |
| more reliable than | /mɔːr rɪˈlaɪəbəl ðən/ |
| better than | /ˈbetər ðən/ → "BE-rer-then" (flap) |
| worse than | /wɝːs ðən/ |

**Práctica:** *"It's a bit slower than the old one, but it's far more maintainable and much easier to debug."* — dila enlazando *than* con la palabra siguiente sin pausa.

### 9 · Practice

**Ejercicio 8A — Comparativo o superlativo.**
1. Postgres is ____ (simple) than DynamoDB to operate.
2. This is ____ (bad) outage we've had this year.
3. gRPC is ____ (fast) than REST, but it's ____ (hard) to debug.
4. It's one of ____ (common) mistakes in distributed systems.
5. Kafka is ____ (reliable) than what we had before.
6. That's ____ (good) approach, but it's also ____ (expensive).
7. This test is ____ (flaky) than the other one.
8. The ____ (little) risky option is usually the boring one.

**Ejercicio 8B — Añade el modificador correcto (much, slightly, twice as, far, too, enough).**
1. It's ____ more expensive — about 3x the cost.
2. It's ____ slower, maybe 5%.
3. It's ____ fast ____ Redis. *(el doble de rápido)*
4. Postgres is fast ____ for our volume.
5. Microservices would be ____ risky for a team of four.

**Ejercicio 8C — Corrige.**
1. *It's more better.*
2. *Postgres is more simple than Mongo.*
3. *It's more faster that the old one.*
4. *It's very faster.*
5. *It's the most fast option.*
6. *This approach is different than the previous one.*
7. *It's not so flexible as SQL.*
8. *I'd rather to use Postgres.*
9. *It's one of the most common mistake.*
10. *It's enough fast.*

**Ejercicio 8D — Traduce.**
1. Postgres es más simple de operar, pero DynamoDB es más rápido para este patrón de acceso.
2. Es bastante más caro con nuestro volumen.
3. El problema mayor es que es mucho menos flexible.
4. ¿Cuánto más rápido es? ¿El doble o diez veces?
5. Postgres es suficientemente rápido para lo que necesitamos.
6. La opción menos arriesgada suele ser la que el equipo ya conoce.
7. Preferiría un sistema algo más lento que podamos depurar.
8. El compromiso es simplicidad operativa frente a latencia pura.

### 10 · Challenge

**Reto 16.** Escribe una comparación real entre dos tecnologías que uses, en 10–12 frases. Requisitos: cuatro comparativos, un superlativo, un *as…as*, tres modificadores de grado distintos, y una conclusión con *I'd lean towards…*

**Reto 17 (oral, 2 min).** Defiende en voz alta una decisión técnica que hayas tomado. Estructura: contexto → opciones → criterios → trade-off → decisión → cuándo la revisarías. Grábate. Esto es literalmente una pregunta de entrevista de nivel senior.

### 11 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto |
|---|---|
| *more easy / more simple* | eas**ier** / **simpler** |
| *most best* | the best |
| *than* vs *that* | comparativo → **than** |
| *It's very better.* | It's **much** better. |
| *I prefer Postgres than Mongo.* | I prefer Postgres **to** Mongo. |
| *It's the same than before.* | the same **as** before |
| *different to* | different **from** |
| *He is major than me.* | He's **older** than me. / more senior |
| *It's more good.* | It's **better**. |
| *as fast as possible* ✅ pero *as fast that possible* ❌ | |

```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```


## UNIT 9 — Estimates, plans and promises

**Nivel:** A2.3 · **Escenario:** sprint planning. Te preguntan cuánto vas a tardar y tienes que comprometerte sin mentir.
**Meta comunicativa:** predecir, prometer, estimar y protegerte con lenguaje probabilístico.
**Gramática:** *will* · *going to* revisado · oraciones temporales de futuro · *may/might* · primer condicional (introducción).

### 1 · Input

> **PRIYA:** How long do you think the migration will take?
> **YOU:** Honestly, it's hard to say. If everything goes well, I'd say three days. But there's a risk that the backfill takes longer — I won't know until I test it with real data volumes.
> **PRIYA:** Can you give me a range?
> **YOU:** Three to five days. I'll have a much better estimate once I've run it against a copy of production.
> **PRIYA:** When will you know?
> **YOU:** By Wednesday. I'm going to set up the test environment tomorrow, so as soon as that's ready I'll run the first pass.
> **PRIYA:** OK. And if it takes longer than five days?
> **YOU:** Then we'll probably need to split it — do the read path first and the write path next sprint. I might be wrong, but I think that's the safest option.
> **PRIYA:** Fine. Let's plan for four days and I'll flag the risk to the PO.

### 2 · Notice — *will*

**Estructura:** *will* + infinitivo (invariable en todas las personas). Contracción **'ll**, negativo **won't** /woʊnt/.

| Uso | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| **Predicción / opinión sobre el futuro** | *I think it'll take three days.* |
| **Decisión tomada en el momento de hablar** | *OK, I'll take a look.* |
| **Promesa u ofrecimiento** | *I'll send you the logs after the call.* |
| **Hecho futuro objetivo** | *The maintenance window will start at 2 a.m.* |
| **Negativa / rechazo** | *It won't work.* |

**La distinción práctica que debes automatizar:**

| Situación | Correcto |
|---|---|
| Alguien te pide algo ahora mismo | *Sure, I'**ll** do it.* (nunca *I'm going to do it*) |
| Ya lo tenías planeado | *I'**m going to** do it this afternoon.* |
| Está en el calendario | *I'**m doing** it at 4.* |
| Es una predicción con evidencia | *It'**s going to** fail.* |
| Es una predicción con opinión | *I think it'**ll** fail.* |

### 3 · Notice — oraciones temporales de futuro (la regla que casi nadie sabe)

**Después de *when, as soon as, before, after, until, once, by the time*, NO se usa *will*. Se usa presente.**

| ❌ | ✅ |
|---|---|
| *I'll deploy when the tests will pass.* | *I'll deploy **when the tests pass**.* |
| *Call me as soon as you will finish.* | *Call me **as soon as you finish**.* |
| *I won't merge until he will approve it.* | *I won't merge **until he approves** it.* |
| *Once it will be green, I'll release.* | ***Once it's green**, I'll release.* |

La razón: en inglés el marcador de futuro aparece **una sola vez**, en la cláusula principal. Es exactamente el mismo principio que "el pasado se marca una vez" de la Unidad 6.

> El español dice "cuando **pasen** los tests" (subjuntivo). El inglés dice *"when the tests **pass**"* (presente). El inglés moderno no tiene subjuntivo funcional, así que usa presente donde el español usa subjuntivo.

**Conectores temporales de futuro:**

| Conector | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| **when** | *I'll ping you when it's done.* |
| **as soon as** | *As soon as CI is green, I'll merge.* |
| **once** | *Once we've deployed, we'll monitor for an hour.* |
| **before / after** | *Before we deploy, let's check the rollback plan.* |
| **until** | *Don't merge until I've reviewed it.* |
| **by the time** | *By the time you're back, it'll be live.* |
| **while** | *While that runs, I'll write the docs.* |
| **the moment** | *I'll let you know the moment I hear back.* |

### 4 · Notice — probabilidad y *hedging* (protegerte con lenguaje)

Un ingeniero que dice *"it will take three days"* y tarda seis pierde credibilidad. Un ingeniero que dice *"it should take around three days, but there's a risk the backfill is slower"* y tarda seis mantiene la confianza. La diferencia no es técnica: es lingüística.

**Escala de certeza:**

| Certeza | Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| 100% | **will** | *The window will open at 2 a.m.* |
| ~90% | **should** | *It should be done by Friday.* |
| ~70% | **will probably / I expect** | *It'll probably take three days.* |
| ~50% | **may / might / could** | *It might take longer.* |
| ~30% | **may not / might not** | *It might not be ready.* |
| ~10% | **is unlikely to** | *It's unlikely to be ready today.* |
| 0% | **won't** | *It won't be ready today.* |

**Suavizadores de estimación (memoriza estos ocho):**

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **Roughly / around / about** three days | aproximación |
| **Three to five days** | rango — la mejor respuesta a una estimación |
| **Best case… worst case…** | escenarios |
| **It's hard to say, but…** | admitir incertidumbre sin evadir |
| **I'd say…** | opinión suavizada |
| **Off the top of my head, …** | sin haberlo pensado a fondo |
| **Don't hold me to this, but…** | no me lo tomes como compromiso |
| **I'll have a better estimate once I've…** | prometer precisión futura |
| **Assuming nothing goes wrong…** | condición implícita |
| **There's a risk that…** | señalar riesgo |
| **That's a rough estimate.** | |
| **Let's plan for X and flag the risk.** | cerrar profesionalmente |

### 5 · Notice — primer condicional (introducción)

**Estructura:** *If* + **presente simple**, → *will* + infinitivo.

- *If the tests pass, I'**ll** merge it.*
- *If it takes longer than five days, we'**ll** split it.*
- *If you give me access, I'**ll** debug it today.*

**Variantes:**
- *If it fails, **we should** revert.* (consejo)
- *If it fails, **we can** revert.* (posibilidad)
- *If it fails, **we might have to** revert.* (probabilidad)
- ***Unless** the tests pass, I won't merge.* (= if…not)
- *I'll merge it, **as long as / provided that** CI is green.* (condición)
- *In case it fails, keep the old version around.* (por si acaso)

> ❌ *If the tests will pass* — misma regla que las temporales: nunca *will* después de *if* en primer condicional.
> ❌ *If it would fail…* — el condicional se marca en la otra cláusula.

### 6 · Sound

**Foco:** *'ll* y *won't*.

| Escrito | IPA | Trampa |
|---|---|---|
| I'll | /aɪl/ | una sílaba, casi como *aisle* |
| it'll | /ˈɪtəl/ | "I-rl" con flap |
| we'll | /wiːl/ | ojo: suena como *wheel* |
| that'll | /ˈðætəl/ | |
| won't | /woʊnt/ | ¡diptongo /oʊ/! No /wɑːnt/ (eso es *want*) |
| want | /wɑːnt/ o /wʌnt/ | |
| will | /wɪl/ | /ɪ/ corta |

> **Par mínimo crítico:** *won't* /woʊnt/ vs *want* /wɑːnt/. *"I won't do it"* (no lo haré) vs *"I want to do it"* (quiero hacerlo). Confundirlos invierte el mensaje.

### 7 · Practice

**Ejercicio 9A — *will*, *going to* o presente continuo.**
1. — The pipeline is red. — I ____ (check) it right now.
2. I ____ (start) the migration tomorrow. I decided yesterday.
3. We ____ (have) a retro on Friday at 3. It's on the calendar.
4. Look at the disk usage — it ____ (run out) tonight.
5. I think this approach ____ (be) easier to maintain.
6. I promise I ____ (not merge) without a review.

**Ejercicio 9B — Presente o *will*.**
1. I'll merge it as soon as the tests ____ (pass).
2. When you ____ (finish), let me know and I ____ (deploy) it.
3. I won't release until Priya ____ (approve) it.
4. By the time we ____ (be) done, it ____ (be) midnight.
5. Once the migration ____ (be) complete, we ____ (delete) the old table.
6. If it ____ (take) longer than a week, we ____ (split) it.

**Ejercicio 9C — Reescribe con *hedging*.** Suaviza estas afirmaciones demasiado rotundas.
1. *It will take three days.*
2. *It will work.*
3. *That's wrong.*
4. *We can't do it this sprint.*
5. *The bug is in the cache layer.*

**Ejercicio 9D — Corrige.**
1. *I will tell you when I will know.*
2. *If it will fail, we revert.*
3. *I'm going to check it right now.* *(alguien te lo acaba de pedir)*
4. *Unless the tests don't pass, I won't merge.*
5. *I won't to do it.*
6. *When I will finish, I'll ping you.*
7. *It's going to take probably three days.* *(orden del adverbio)*
8. *I'll can do it tomorrow.*

**Ejercicio 9E — Traduce.**
1. Sinceramente, es difícil decirlo.
2. Si todo va bien, diría tres días.
3. Hay riesgo de que el backfill tarde más.
4. No lo sabré hasta que lo pruebe con volúmenes reales.
5. ¿Puedes darme un rango? — De tres a cinco días.
6. Tendré una estimación mucho mejor cuando lo haya ejecutado contra una copia de producción.
7. En cuanto esté listo, ejecutaré la primera pasada.
8. Puede que me equivoque, pero creo que es la opción más segura.

### 8 · Challenge

**Reto 18.** Escribe tu respuesta real a *"How long will this take?"* para una tarea que tengas ahora mismo. Requisitos: un rango, un riesgo explícito, una condición con *if*, una oración temporal de futuro sin *will*, y un compromiso concreto de cuándo darás mejor información.

**Reto 19.** Reescribe la misma estimación tres veces: (a) para tu tech lead, (b) para el product owner, (c) para un cliente externo. Observa qué cambia: no la gramática, sino el grado de *hedging* y el nivel de detalle técnico.

### 9 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto |
|---|---|
| *When I will finish…* | When I **finish**… |
| *If it will fail…* | If it **fails**… |
| *I will can…* | I **will be able to**… |
| *I won't* /wɑːnt/ | pronuncia /woʊnt/ |
| *I'm going to help you* (respondiendo a una petición) | I'**ll** help you |
| *It will take me 3 days to make it* | …**to do** it / **to get it done** |
| *I hope to have it for Friday.* | I hope to have it **by** Friday. |
| *Until Friday it will be ready.* | It'll be ready **by** Friday. |
| *I'll inform you.* | I'**ll let you know**. (más natural) |
| *Probably it will take…* | It'll **probably** take… |

```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```


## UNIT 10 — Your experience: present perfect vs past simple

**Nivel:** A2.3 · **Escenario:** entrevista, 1:1 de carrera, o simplemente explicar qué has hecho antes.
**Meta comunicativa:** hablar de experiencia acumulada sin fechas, y distinguirla de hechos cerrados con fecha.
**Gramática:** presente perfecto · *for* / *since* · *ever* / *never* / *just* / *already* / *yet* · presente perfecto continuo · contraste con pasado simple.

> **Aviso.** Esta es la unidad más importante del libro para un hispanohablante. El presente perfecto inglés **no** equivale al pretérito perfecto español, aunque se parezcan. El 80% de los hispanohablantes de nivel B1 sigue cometiendo estos errores. Dedícale el doble de tiempo.

### 1 · Input

> **INTERVIEWER:** Tell me a bit about your background.
> **YOU:** Sure. I've been a backend developer for about six years. I've worked mostly with Java and Go, and for the last two years I've been focusing on distributed systems.
> **INTERVIEWER:** Have you ever led a project?
> **YOU:** Yes, I have. I led the payments migration at my current company — that was in 2024. It took eight months and we moved about 40 million records.
> **INTERVIEWER:** Have you worked with Kafka?
> **YOU:** I have, but not in production. I've used it in a side project and I've read a lot about it, but I haven't operated it at scale.
> **INTERVIEWER:** And how long have you been at your current company?
> **YOU:** Three years. I joined in 2023 as a mid-level engineer and I've been a senior since last year.
> **INTERVIEWER:** Have you had any experience with on-call?
> **YOU:** Yes — I've been on the on-call rotation since 2024. I've handled maybe fifteen incidents. The worst one was a cascading failure last March.

### 2 · Notice — la regla que lo resuelve todo

**Estructura del presente perfecto:** *have / has* + **participio pasado**.

| Sujeto | | |
|---|---|---|
| I / you / we / they | **have** worked | **I've** worked |
| he / she / it | **has** worked | **he's** worked |
| Negativo | **haven't / hasn't** worked | |
| Interrogativo | **Have** you worked…? | |

**La regla única:**

> **Si la frase contiene o implica un momento pasado terminado y concreto → PASADO SIMPLE.**
> **Si no hay momento concreto, o el periodo sigue abierto → PRESENTE PERFECTO.**

| Presente perfecto (sin cuándo) | Pasado simple (con cuándo) |
|---|---|
| *I'**ve** work**ed** with Kafka.* | *I work**ed** with Kafka **in 2022**.* |
| *I'**ve** l**ed** two migrations.* | *I l**ed** the payments migration **last year**.* |
| *We'**ve** fix**ed** the bug.* | *We fix**ed** it **yesterday**.* |
| *Have you **ever** been on call?* | *Were you on call **last week**?* |
| *He'**s** join**ed** the team.* | *He join**ed** **in March**.* |

**Test práctico:** ¿puedes añadir *"yesterday"*, *"in 2023"*, *"last week"* sin que la frase suene rara? Si sí → pasado simple.

### 3 · Los cuatro usos del presente perfecto

**Uso 1 — Experiencia de vida, sin especificar cuándo.**
*I've worked with three different message brokers.* · *I've never used Rust in production.*
Marcadores: *ever, never, before, once, twice, a few times, several times*.

**Uso 2 — Acción pasada con resultado presente relevante.**
*I've fixed it.* (→ ahora funciona) · *Someone has deleted the index.* (→ el índice no está)
*The build has failed.* (→ está roja ahora)

**Uso 3 — Situación que empezó en el pasado y CONTINÚA.** ← aquí está tu error principal
*I've been here **for** three years.* (y sigo aquí)
*We've used Postgres **since** 2019.* (y lo seguimos usando)

**Uso 4 — Acción reciente sin fecha.**
*I've **just** deployed it.* · *Have you finished **yet**?* · *I've **already** reviewed it.*

### 4 · *for* vs *since* — memoriza esto como una constante

| | Significado | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| **for** | **duración** (cuánto tiempo) | *for three years*, *for two hours*, *for a while*, *for ages* |
| **since** | **punto de inicio** (desde cuándo) | *since 2019*, *since March*, *since Monday*, *since I joined*, *since the last release* |

| ❌ | ✅ |
|---|---|
| *I work here since 2023.* | *I'**ve worked** here **since** 2023.* |
| *I'm working here for 3 years.* | *I'**ve been working** here **for** 3 years.* |
| *I have 3 years here.* | *I'**ve been** here **for** 3 years.* |
| *Since 3 years* | **For** 3 years |
| *How long time do you work here?* | *How long **have** you **worked** here?* |

> **La causa del error:** el español usa **presente** para esto ("trabajo aquí desde 2023", "llevo tres años aquí"). El inglés usa **obligatoriamente** presente perfecto. Si dices *"I work here since 2023"*, un nativo entiende algo confuso y sabe inmediatamente que eres hispanohablante o francófono.

**Otras formas de expresar duración:**

| Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| *It's been* + tiempo + *since* | *It's been three years since I joined.* |
| *How long has it been since…?* | |
| *I've had this laptop for two years.* | |
| *We haven't deployed since Tuesday.* | |

### 5 · Adverbios del presente perfecto

| Adverbio | Posición | Uso | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|---|
| **ever** | entre *have* y participio | preguntas de experiencia | *Have you **ever** used Rust?* |
| **never** | idem | experiencia negativa | *I've **never** used Rust.* |
| **just** | idem | acaba de ocurrir | *I've **just** deployed it.* |
| **already** | idem (o final) | antes de lo esperado | *I've **already** reviewed it.* |
| **yet** | **al final**, solo ?/- | esperado pero no ocurrido | *Have you finished **yet**?* / *I haven't finished **yet**.* |
| **still** | antes del verbo, en negativas | continúa sin ocurrir | *I **still** haven't heard back.* |
| **so far / up to now** | inicio o final | hasta ahora | *So far it's been stable.* |
| **recently / lately** | final | | *It's been failing a lot lately.* |
| **once / twice / three times** | final | frecuencia | *I've seen this twice.* |

> ⚠️ *already* vs *yet*: *already* es afirmativo ("ya"), *yet* es negativo/interrogativo ("todavía / ya"). ❌ *I've already not finished.* ✅ *I haven't finished **yet**.*
> ⚠️ En inglés americano informal se oye *"Did you finish yet?"* con pasado simple. Es aceptado en habla US; en escritura usa el perfecto.

### 6 · Presente perfecto continuo

**Estructura:** *have / has been* + verbo**-ing**.

| Uso | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| Duración de una actividad **en curso** | *I'**ve been debugging** this for three hours.* |
| Actividad reciente con efecto visible | *Sorry, I'**ve been running** load tests — that's why the staging DB is slow.* |
| Enfatizar el **proceso** más que el resultado | *We'**ve been working** on it all week.* |

**Perfecto simple vs perfecto continuo:**

| Simple (resultado) | Continuo (proceso/duración) |
|---|---|
| *I'**ve fixed** three bugs today.* (cuántos) | *I'**ve been fixing** bugs all day.* (actividad) |
| *I'**ve read** the RFC.* (terminado) | *I'**ve been reading** the RFC.* (en ello) |
| *He'**s written** the docs.* (existen) | *He'**s been writing** docs all morning.* (dedicación) |

> Con verbos de estado (*be, know, have*) usa el **simple**: ✅ *I've **known** him for years.* ❌ *I've been knowing him.*
> Con *for* + duración, el continuo es lo más natural para actividades: *I've been working here for three years.* También es correcto el simple: *I've worked here for three years.* Prácticamente intercambiables.

### 7 · Sound

**Foco:** las contracciones del perfecto, que son casi inaudibles y por eso no las produces.

| Escrito | IPA | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| I've | /aɪv/ | una sílaba con /v/ real |
| you've | /juːv/ | |
| we've | /wiːv/ | |
| they've | /ðeɪv/ | |
| he's (= he has) | /hiːz/ | igual que *he is* — el contexto decide |
| it's (= it has) | /ɪts/ | *It's failed.* = it has failed |
| haven't | /ˈhævənt/ | |
| hasn't | /ˈhæzənt/ | la *s* es /z/ |
| I've been | /aɪv bɪn/ | *been* es /bɪn/ átono, no /biːn/ |
| I'd | /aɪd/ | = I had o I would |

**Trampa auditiva:** en habla rápida *"I've been"* suena /aɪvbɪn/, casi /aɪbɪn/. Si no lo reconoces, oyes solo *"I been working here for three years"* y no sabes qué tiempo verbal es. Entrena esta secuencia:

*"I've been working here for three years and I've never seen it fail like this."*
/aɪv bɪn ˈwɝːkɪŋ hɪr fər ˈθriː jɪrz ənd aɪv ˈnevər ˈsiːn ɪt ˈfeɪl ˈlaɪk ˈðɪs/

**Participios con trampa fonética:** *read* /red/, *written* /ˈrɪtən/ (flap: "RI-rn"), *done* /dʌn/, *gone* /ɡɔːn/, *seen* /siːn/, *been* /bɪn/, *known* /noʊn/, *chosen* /ˈtʃoʊzən/, *broken* /ˈbroʊkən/, *taken* /ˈteɪkən/.

### 8 · Chunks

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **Tell me a bit about your background.** | pregunta de apertura de entrevista |
| **I've been a backend developer for six years.** | |
| **I've worked mostly with…** | |
| **For the last two years I've been focusing on…** | |
| **Have you ever…?** | |
| **I have, but not in production.** | matizar experiencia |
| **I've used it in a side project.** | |
| **I haven't operated it at scale.** | admitir límite con precisión |
| **I've been on the on-call rotation since 2024.** | |
| **I've handled about fifteen incidents.** | |
| **I've never had to do that before.** | |
| **That's the first time I've seen that.** | |
| **We've come a long way.** | hemos avanzado mucho |
| **So far, so good.** | |
| **I've just pushed a fix.** | |
| **Has anyone looked at this yet?** | |

### 9 · Practice

**Ejercicio 10A — Presente perfecto o pasado simple.**
1. I ____ (work) with Kafka, but not in production.
2. I ____ (lead) the payments migration in 2024.
3. ____ you ever ____ (be) on call?
4. He ____ (join) the team in March.
5. He ____ (be) on the team since March.

7. ____ you ____ (finish) the review yet?

10. The build ____ (fail) — someone needs to look at it.
11. How long ____ you ____ (be) at this company?
12. When ____ you ____ (start) here?

**Ejercicio 10B — *for* o *since*.**

5. I've known him ____ we worked together at my last job.

4. I've ____ reviewed it, you can merge.

**Ejercicio 10D — Presente perfecto simple o continuo.**

4. I ____ (know) him for years.

**Ejercicio 10E — Corrige.** (Todos son errores reales de hispanohablantes.)

2. *I'm working here for three years.*

### 10 · Challenge

**Reto 20.** Escribe tu "background" de entrevista en 8–10 frases. Requisitos: tres presentes perfectos, dos pasados simples con fecha explícita, un *for*, un *since*, un presente perfecto continuo, y una limitación admitida con honestidad (*I haven't…*).

**Reto 21 (oral, 90 segundos).** Grábate respondiendo *"Tell me about your background."* Escúchate y cuenta cuántas veces has dicho *"I work here since…"* o similar. Cada aparición es una tarjeta de repaso espaciado.

**Reto 22.** Reescribe tu perfil de LinkedIn o la sección de experiencia de tu CV aplicando la regla: descripciones de trabajos actuales en presente perfecto o presente simple; logros pasados y cerrados en pasado simple con cifras.

### 11 · Trampas del hispanohablante — las diez definitivas

| Error | Correcto | Por qué |
|---|---|---|
| *I work here since 2023.* | I'**ve worked** here since 2023. | situación que continúa → perfecto |
| *I'm working here for 3 years.* | I'**ve been working** here for 3 years. | idem |
| *I have 3 years in this company.* | I'**ve been** at this company for 3 years. | *tener años* ≠ *have* |
| *Since 3 years* | **For** 3 years | *since* = punto, *for* = duración |
| *I've deployed it yesterday.* | I deploy**ed** it yesterday. | fecha concreta → pasado simple |
| *He has joined last March.* | He join**ed** last March. | idem |
| *Did you finish it already?* | Have you finish**ed** it **yet**? | (US informal lo permite) |
| *I didn't see him today.* | I **haven't seen** him today. | *today* sigue abierto |
| *Have you ever went…?* | Have you ever **been**…? | participio, no pasado |
| *I've never been knowing…* | I'**ve never known**… | verbo de estado → simple |

## UNIT 11 — Writing instructions and documentation

**Nivel:** A2.4 · **Escenario:** escribes un README, los pasos de una migración, un runbook o la descripción de un PR.
**Meta comunicativa:** dar instrucciones claras, ordenadas y sin ambigüedad.
**Gramática:** imperativo · secuenciadores · voz pasiva (introducción) · *should / must / need to* · condicionales de instrucción.

### 1 · Input

> **README.md — Local setup**
>
> ## Prerequisites
> Before you start, make sure you have Docker 24+ and Node 20 installed. You'll also need access to the internal npm registry.
>
> ## Steps
> 1. Clone the repository and check out the `develop` branch.
> 2. Copy `.env.example` to `.env` and fill in the values. **Do not** commit this file.
> 3. Run `docker compose up -d` to start Postgres and Redis.
> 4. Once the containers are healthy, run the migrations with `npm run migrate`.
> 5. Start the dev server with `npm run dev`. The app should be available at `http://localhost:3000`.
>
> ## Troubleshooting
> If the migrations fail with a connection error, check that Postgres is actually running: `docker compose ps`. The most common cause is a port conflict — port 5432 may already be in use by a local Postgres instance.
>
> **Note:** the seed data is generated automatically the first time you run the app. This can take up to two minutes, so be patient.
>
> If you get stuck, ping `#team-payments` on Slack.

### 2 · Notice — el imperativo

En documentación, el imperativo es **correcto y esperado**. La regla de cortesía de la Unidad 5 aplica a **personas**, no a **instrucciones escritas**.

**Estructura:** verbo en infinitivo sin sujeto.
*Clone the repo.* · *Run the migrations.* · *Check the logs.*

**Negativo:** *Do not* + verbo (en documentación se prefiere la forma completa a *don't*).
*Do not commit secrets.* · *Never run this in production.*

**Con *please*** solo si te diriges a una persona concreta, no en docs genéricas.

**Registro de documentación: cuatro estilos y cuándo usarlos.**

| Estilo | Ejemplo | Uso |
|---|---|---|
| Imperativo (recomendado) | *Run the migration.* | pasos accionables |
| *You* + presente | *You run the migration.* / *You'll need to…* | tutoriales, tono cercano |
| Pasiva | *The migration is run automatically.* | describir lo que hace el sistema |
| Gerundio como título | *Running the migration* | encabezados de sección |

> **Convención profesional:** en documentación técnica en inglés, usa **imperativo para instrucciones** y **presente simple para describir comportamiento del sistema**. Evita el futuro (*"the system will then validate"* → *"the system validates"*).

### 3 · Notice — secuenciadores y conectores de instrucción

| Función | Conectores |
|---|---|
| **Inicio** | First, / To begin, / Start by + -ing |
| **Continuación** | Then, / Next, / After that, / Once that's done, |
| **Simultaneidad** | While that runs, / Meanwhile, / At the same time, |
| **Precedencia** | Before you + verbo, / Prior to + -ing |
| **Posterioridad** | After you + verbo, / Once you've + participio, |
| **Final** | Finally, / Lastly, / To finish, |
| **Condición** | If…, / In case…, / Unless…, / Should + sujeto (formal) |
| **Advertencia** | Note that… / Be aware that… / Make sure… / Bear in mind that… |
| **Excepción** | Except for… / Other than… / Apart from… |
| **Alternativa** | Alternatively, / Otherwise, / Instead, |
| **Resultado** | As a result, / This means that… / Consequently, |

**Trampas de conectores muy frecuentes:**

| ❌ | ✅ | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| *At the end, run the tests.* | **Finally**, run the tests. | *at the end* = al final físico de algo |
| *Actually, run npm install.* | **First**, run npm install. | *actually* = "en realidad" |
| *In the other hand* | **On the other hand** | |
| *For finish* | **To finish** / Finally | |
| *After to run it* | **After running it** / After you run it | tras preposición → *-ing* |
| *Before to deploy* | **Before deploying** / Before you deploy | |
| *Make sure to have Docker* | **Make sure you have** Docker | |
| *Take in account* | **Take into account** / Bear in mind | |

### 4 · Notice — voz pasiva (introducción)

**Estructura:** *be* + participio pasado.

| Activa | Pasiva |
|---|---|
| *The system validates the token.* | *The token **is validated** by the system.* |
| *We deploy the app automatically.* | *The app **is deployed** automatically.* |
| *Someone deleted the index.* | *The index **was deleted**.* |
| *You must approve the PR.* | *The PR **must be approved**.* |

**Cuándo usar pasiva en documentación técnica:**
1. Cuando el agente es **irrelevante u obvio**: *The migrations are run on startup.*
2. Cuando el agente es **desconocido**: *The record was deleted at 3 a.m.*
3. Cuando quieres poner el foco en **el objeto**: *Secrets are stored in Vault.*
4. Cuando quieres evitar culpar a alguien: *A production index was deleted.* (en lugar de *"Maya deleted…"*)

> El punto 4 es de uso diario en postmortems y merece su propia sección en la Unidad 16.

**Formas pasivas frecuentes en docs:**
*is generated · is validated · is cached · is stored · is triggered · is logged · is retried · is rate-limited · is deprecated · must be approved · should be avoided · can be configured · is not supported*

### 5 · Notice — obligación y recomendación

| Modal | Fuerza | Uso en docs |
|---|---|---|
| **must** | obligación absoluta | *All requests must include an auth token.* |
| **must not** | prohibición | *You must not commit `.env`.* |
| **have to / need to** | obligación práctica | *You'll need to request access first.* |
| **should** | recomendación fuerte | *You should run the tests locally first.* |
| **shouldn't** | no recomendado | *You shouldn't use this in production.* |
| **can** | posibilidad/permiso | *You can override this with `--force`.* |
| **may** | posibilidad | *This may take up to two minutes.* |
| **don't have to / don't need to** | **no es necesario** | *You don't have to restart the server.* |

> ⚠️ **La distinción más importante:** *mustn't* = **prohibido**. *don't have to* = **no es necesario**. No son sinónimos y confundirlos en documentación es peligroso.
> *You **mustn't** delete the table.* (prohibido)
> *You **don't have to** delete the table.* (opcional)

### 6 · Vocabulario de documentación

| Inglés | Español |
|---|---|
| prerequisites | requisitos previos |
| setup / installation | |
| troubleshooting | resolución de problemas |
| known issues | problemas conocidos |
| caveats | advertencias, limitaciones |
| out of scope | fuera de alcance |
| by default | por defecto |
| out of the box | sin configuración extra |
| under the hood | internamente |
| edge case | caso límite |
| workaround | solución temporal |
| breaking change | cambio incompatible |
| deprecated | obsoleto |
| backwards compatible | retrocompatible |
| rollback plan | plan de reversión |
| dry run | ejecución de prueba |
| smoke test | prueba superficial |
| happy path | camino ideal |
| boilerplate | código repetitivo de base |
| placeholder | valor de relleno |
| snippet | fragmento de código |
| toggle / feature flag | interruptor de funcionalidad |
| throttling / rate limiting | limitación de tasa |
| idempotent | idempotente |
| stale | obsoleto, caducado (datos) |
| fallback | alternativa de respaldo |
| overhead | sobrecarga |
| nested | anidado |
| whitelist / allowlist | lista de permitidos |

### 7 · Sound

**Foco:** los verbos de instrucción y los conectores llevan acento en la primera sílaba del enunciado. Practica leyendo el README del Input en voz alta, marcando la pausa después de cada conector:

*"First, /// clone the repository. /// Then, /// copy the env file. /// Once that's done, /// run the migrations."*

Las comas después de *First*, *Then*, *Finally* son **pausas reales**, no decorativas. Un hispanohablante las lee de corrido y el resultado suena atropellado.

### 8 · Practice

**Ejercicio 11A — Convierte a imperativo.**
1. *You have to clone the repository.*
2. *It's necessary to copy the env file.*
3. *The user should not commit secrets.*
4. *You need to run the migrations before starting the server.*

**Ejercicio 11B — Activa a pasiva.**
1. *The system validates the token.*
2. *Someone deleted the production index.*
3. *We store secrets in Vault.*
4. *You must approve every PR.*
5. *The pipeline runs the tests automatically.*

**Ejercicio 11C — *must*, *mustn't*, *don't have to*, *should*.**
1. You ____ commit the `.env` file. *(prohibido)*
2. You ____ restart the server — it reloads automatically. *(no es necesario)*
3. All requests ____ include an auth token. *(obligatorio)*
4. You ____ run the tests locally first. *(recomendado)*
5. You ____ use `--force` unless you know what you're doing. *(no recomendado)*

**Ejercicio 11D — Corrige los conectores.**
1. *At the end, restart the service.*
2. *Actually, install the dependencies.*
3. *After to run the migration, start the server.*
4. *In the other hand, you can use the CLI.*
5. *Make sure to have Docker installed.*
6. *Take in account that this takes two minutes.*
7. *For finish, run the smoke tests.*
8. *Before to deploy, check the rollback plan.*

**Ejercicio 11E — Traduce (registro de documentación).**
1. Antes de empezar, asegúrate de tener Docker 24 o superior instalado.
2. Copia `.env.example` a `.env` y rellena los valores. No subas este archivo al repositorio.
3. Una vez que los contenedores estén sanos, ejecuta las migraciones.
4. Los secretos se almacenan en Vault, no en el repositorio.
5. Los datos de prueba se generan automáticamente la primera vez que ejecutas la app.
6. Si las migraciones fallan con un error de conexión, comprueba que Postgres esté ejecutándose.
7. La causa más habitual es un conflicto de puertos.
8. Esto puede tardar hasta dos minutos, así que ten paciencia.

### 9 · Challenge

**Reto 23.** Escribe el README de configuración local de tu proyecto real, en inglés, con cuatro secciones: *Prerequisites*, *Steps* (mínimo cinco pasos numerados en imperativo), *Troubleshooting* (dos problemas con causa y solución), *Notes*.

**Reto 24.** Escribe la descripción de tu último pull request con esta plantilla:
> **What** — qué cambia (una frase en presente simple).
> **Why** — por qué (una o dos frases; enlaza el ticket).
> **How** — enfoque técnico (2–3 frases).
> **Testing** — qué has probado y cómo.
> **Risks / rollback** — qué puede salir mal y cómo revertirlo.

### 10 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto |
|---|---|
| *For install it, run npm i.* | **To install** it, run npm i. |
| *Is necessary to restart.* | **You need to** restart. / **It's necessary to** restart. |
| *Please to run the tests.* | **Run** the tests. / **Please run** the tests. |
| *Depending of the config* | Depending **on** the config |
| *In case of the test fails* | **In case the test fails** / **If** the test fails |
| *Explain me how it works.* | **Explain to me** how it works. / Explain how it works. |
| *Advice* (verbo) | **advise** /ədˈvaɪz/ es el verbo; *advice* /ədˈvaɪs/ el sustantivo |
| *Note that the token expire.* | …the token **expires**. |
| *Realize the migration* | **Run / perform / carry out** the migration (*realize* = darse cuenta) |
| *Assist to the meeting* | **attend** the meeting (*assist* = ayudar) |

```{=openxml}

```


## UNIT 12 — Narrating a bug: what was happening when it broke

**Nivel:** A2.4 · **Escenario:** explicas a tu equipo qué estaba pasando cuando el sistema falló.
**Meta comunicativa:** narrar con fondo y primer plano; contrastar cómo eran las cosas antes.
**Gramática:** pasado continuo · *when* / *while* · *used to* / *would* · *There was/were*.

### 1 · Input

> **PRIYA:** So what happened exactly?
> **YOU:** We were running the batch job when the database ran out of connections. The job was processing about 5,000 records a minute, and while it was running, the API started timing out.
> **TOM:** Was anything else happening at the same time?
> **YOU:** Yes — that's the thing. Someone was running a manual backfill in another session. Neither job knew about the other.
> **PRIYA:** How did you notice?
> **YOU:** I wasn't looking at the dashboards, actually. A customer complained. By the time I checked, the connection pool was already saturated.
> **TOM:** We used to have an alert for that.
> **YOU:** We did. It was removed last year because it was too noisy. We used to get about twenty false positives a week.
> **PRIYA:** Right. So the fix is to bring the alert back with a better threshold, and to add a lock so that two backfills can't run at once.
> **YOU:** That's my read too.

### 2 · Notice — pasado continuo

**Estructura:** *was / were* + verbo**-ing**.

| Sujeto | |
|---|---|
| I / he / she / it | **was** running |
| you / we / they | **were** running |
| Negativo | **wasn't / weren't** running |
| Interrogativo | **Was / Were** it running? |

**Tres usos:**

**Uso 1 — Fondo: acción en curso en un momento del pasado.**
*At 3 p.m. the job **was still running**.*

**Uso 2 — Acción larga interrumpida por una corta.** ← el uso central
*We **were running** the batch job **when** the database **ran out** of connections.*
(fondo = continuo · interrupción = pasado simple)

**Uso 3 — Dos acciones simultáneas.**
***While** the job **was running**, the API **started** timing out.*
*Tom **was reviewing** the PR **while** I **was writing** the tests.* (ambas largas)

### 3 · Notice — *when* vs *while*

| | Regla | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| **when** | + acción **corta** (pasado simple) | *I was debugging **when** it crashed.* |
| **while** | + acción **larga** (pasado continuo) | ***While** I was debugging, it crashed.* |

Ambas frases significan lo mismo; cambia qué elemento pones en primer plano.

**El contraste que importa:**

| Frase | Significado |
|---|---|
| *When the alert fired, I **was checking** the logs.* | ya estaba mirando los logs antes de la alerta |
| *When the alert fired, I **checked** the logs.* | miré los logs **después** de la alerta, como reacción |

Esta diferencia decide si en un postmortem quedas como alguien que estaba vigilando o como alguien que reaccionó tarde. Es gramática con consecuencias.

### 4 · Notice — *used to* y *would*

**used to** + infinitivo = hábito o estado **pasado que ya no ocurre**.

| Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| Afirmativo | *We **used to** have an alert for that.* |
| Negativo | *We **didn't use to** deploy on Fridays.* (sin *d*) |
| Interrogativo | ***Did** you **use to** run it manually?* (sin *d*) |

> ⚠️ En negativo e interrogativo se escribe *use to*, sin *-d*, porque el pasado ya está en *did*. Misma regla de siempre.

**would** + infinitivo = hábito pasado **repetido** (solo acciones, no estados).

*Every Friday we **would** freeze the release.* · *He **would** always forget to update the changelog.*

| ✅ *used to* | ✅ *would* | ❌ |
|---|---|---|
| *We used to have three services.* (estado) | | ❌ *We would have three services.* |
| *We used to deploy manually.* (acción) | *We would deploy manually.* | |
| *I used to be a QA engineer.* (estado) | | ❌ *I would be a QA engineer.* |

**Contraste con presente:** para hábitos actuales usa presente simple, no *use to*.
❌ *I use to deploy on Tuesdays.* ✅ *I **usually** deploy on Tuesdays.*

> Este error es frecuentísimo porque en español "suelo desplegar" se parece a *use to*. **No existe** *"I use to"* en presente con ese significado. Usa *usually*.

### 5 · Vocabulario de incidentes

| Inglés | Español |
|---|---|
| outage | caída de servicio |
| downtime | tiempo de inactividad |
| incident / postmortem | |
| root cause | causa raíz |
| trigger | detonante |
| to run out of (connections, memory, disk) | agotarse |
| to saturate / saturated | saturar |
| to spike | dispararse (una métrica) |
| to degrade | degradarse |
| to crash | caerse, romperse |
| to hang | quedarse colgado |
| to time out | expirar por tiempo |
| to throttle | limitar |
| to fail over | conmutar a respaldo |
| to roll back | revertir |
| to mitigate | mitigar |
| blast radius | radio de impacto |
| cascading failure | fallo en cascada |
| false positive | falso positivo |
| noisy alert | alerta ruidosa |
| threshold | umbral |
| connection pool | pool de conexiones |
| memory leak | fuga de memoria |
| race condition | condición de carrera |
| deadlock | interbloqueo |
| bottleneck | cuello de botella |
| to page someone | avisar a alguien (guardia) |
| on-call rotation | rotación de guardias |
| customer-facing | visible para el cliente |
| to escalate | escalar |

### 6 · Sound

**Foco:** *was* y *were* tienen forma fuerte y débil. En narración fluida son **débiles**.

| Palabra | Fuerte | Débil (habitual) |
|---|---|---|
| was | /wɑːz/ | **/wəz/** |
| were | /wɝː/ | **/wər/** |
| wasn't | /ˈwɑːzənt/ | (siempre fuerte, es negativo) |
| weren't | /wɝːnt/ | (siempre fuerte) |

*"We were running the job"* → /wi wər ˈrʌnɪŋ ðə dʒɑːb/ → "wi-wer-RA-ning".

**Regla general de negativos:** en inglés, **las formas negativas nunca se reducen**. Se pronuncian fuertes y claras precisamente porque la negación es información crítica: *wasn't, weren't, didn't, don't, can't, won't, haven't*. Si te cuesta oír si algo es negativo, escucha la **fuerza** de la sílaba, no la /t/ final.

### 7 · Chunks

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **So what happened exactly?** | abrir la investigación |
| **We were running X when Y happened.** | estructura narrativa central |
| **While it was running, …** | |
| **At the same time, …** | |
| **That's the thing.** | ahí está la clave |
| **By the time I checked, …** | cuando llegué a mirar |
| **I wasn't looking at the dashboards.** | admitir |
| **A customer complained.** | |
| **It turned out that…** | resultó que |
| **We used to have an alert for that.** | |
| **It was removed because it was too noisy.** | |
| **That's my read too.** | coincido en la interpretación |
| **It's been happening intermittently.** | |
| **We got lucky.** | tuvimos suerte |
| **It could have been much worse.** | |

### 8 · Practice

**Ejercicio 12A — Pasado simple o pasado continuo.**
1. We ____ (run) the batch job when the database ____ (run out) of connections.
2. While the job ____ (process) records, the API ____ (start) timing out.
3. I ____ (not look) at the dashboards when the alert ____ (fire).
4. What ____ you ____ (do) when it ____ (crash)?
5. Someone ____ (run) a manual backfill in another session.
6. By the time I ____ (check), the pool ____ (already be) saturated.
7. He ____ (review) the PR while I ____ (write) the tests.

**Ejercicio 12B — *used to*, *would*, *usually* o presente simple.**
1. We ____ have an alert for that, but we removed it.
2. I ____ deploy on Tuesdays. *(hábito actual)*
3. Every Friday we ____ freeze the release. *(hábito pasado repetido)*
4. ____ you ____ run the migration manually? *(pregunta sobre el pasado)*
5. We ____ deploy on Fridays. *(pasado, ya no)* — negativo
6. I ____ be a QA engineer before I moved to backend.

**Ejercicio 12C — Corrige.**
1. *I was checking the logs when it was crashing.*
2. *We used to had an alert.*
3. *Did you used to deploy manually?*
4. *I use to deploy on Tuesdays.* *(hábito actual)*
5. *While I debugged, it crashed.*
6. *We didn't used to have monitoring.*
7. *I was wanting to ask about that.*
8. *When the alert fired, I was checked the logs.*

**Ejercicio 12D — Traduce.**
1. Estábamos ejecutando el batch cuando la base de datos se quedó sin conexiones.
2. Mientras se ejecutaba, la API empezó a dar timeouts.
3. ¿Estaba pasando algo más al mismo tiempo?
4. No estaba mirando los dashboards, la verdad.
5. Cuando fui a comprobarlo, el pool ya estaba saturado.
6. Antes teníamos una alerta para eso.
7. Se eliminó porque era demasiado ruidosa.
8. Solíamos recibir unos veinte falsos positivos por semana.

**Ejercicio 12E — Repaso intercalado (U7–U11).**
1. *If the tests will pass, I will merge it.*
2. *I work here since three years.*
3. *It's more simple than the alternative.*
4. *At the end, restart the service.*
5. *Would you mind to take a look?*

### 9 · Challenge

**Reto 25.** Narra un incidente real que hayas vivido, en 10–12 frases. Requisitos: tres pasados continuos, dos *when* y un *while*, un *used to*, tres términos de la tabla de vocabulario de incidentes, y una conclusión sobre la causa raíz.

**Reto 26 (oral, 2 min).** Cuenta ese incidente en voz alta como si estuvieras en la reunión de postmortem. Estructura: *what happened → timeline → impact → root cause → what we're doing about it*. Esta es una habilidad que se evalúa explícitamente en entrevistas de nivel senior y SRE.

### 10 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto |
|---|---|
| *I was checking when it was crashing.* | I was checking when it **crashed**. |
| *We used to had* | We used to **have** |
| *Did you used to…?* | Did you **use to**…? |
| *I use to work late.* (presente) | I **usually** work late. |
| *While I debugged* | While I **was debugging** |
| *It was passing something strange.* | Something strange **was happening**. |
| *I was agreeing with him.* | I **agreed** with him. |
| *Nobody didn't notice.* | **Nobody noticed.** |
| *It failed all the time yesterday.* | It **kept failing** yesterday. |
| *The customer complained about that it was slow.* | The customer complained **that** it was slow. |
