# PARTE 5 — NIVEL B2

> **Objetivo:** al terminar la Unidad 26 podrás pasar una entrevista técnica en inglés, negociar tus condiciones, dar feedback difícil, presentar en público y escribir documentos que persuadan. B2 no es "hablar más": es **elegir** entre varias formas correctas la que produce el efecto que quieres.
>
> **Cambio de exigencia:** en B2 la gramática deja de ser el problema y pasa a ser el instrumento. Cada unidad de esta parte enseña una **estrategia retórica**, no solo una estructura.

## UNIT 19 — Regret, blame and the third conditional

**Nivel:** B2.1 · **Escenario:** la parte más delicada de un postmortem: hablar de lo que se hizo mal sin destruir a nadie, incluido tú mismo.
**Meta comunicativa:** analizar el pasado contrafactual y expresar arrepentimiento con precisión.
**Gramática:** tercer condicional · condicionales mixtos · *wish / if only* · *should have* · *was supposed to*.

### 1 · Input

> **PRIYA:** Let's do the *what could we have done differently* section. And remember — blameless. We're looking for system failures, not people.
>
> **TOM:** If we'd load-tested at production volumes, we would have caught the leak before release. That's the big one.
>
> **MAYA:** Right, and if the alert threshold hadn't been raised in December, we'd have been paged twenty minutes earlier. The impact would have been a fraction of what it was.
>
> **YOU:** I keep coming back to the fact that nobody owned the alert. If it had had an owner, someone would have noticed that it had been silenced. It wasn't anyone's job, which is exactly the kind of gap we should be looking for.
>
> **PRIYA:** Agreed. I wish we'd written down the temporary threshold change as a ticket. It was supposed to be reverted in January — it just never happened.
>
> **TOM:** In hindsight, we should have treated a threshold change as a config change, with the same review process.
>
> **MAYA:** Had we done that, it would have shown up in the December config diff.
>
> **PRIYA:** OK. So the action item isn't *"be more careful"* — it's *"alerts need owners and threshold changes need tickets"*. Those are systemic.

### 2 · Notice — tercer condicional

**Función:** hablar de un pasado **que no ocurrió** y de su consecuencia hipotética. Es el tiempo del análisis retrospectivo, del arrepentimiento y de la crítica constructiva.

**Estructura:** *If* + **had** + participio, → **would have** + participio.

| Realidad | Tercer condicional |
|---|---|
| No hicimos load test. Encontramos el leak tarde. | *If we **had load-tested**, we **would have caught** it earlier.* |
| Subieron el umbral. No sonó la alerta. | *If they **hadn't raised** the threshold, the alert **would have fired**.* |
| La alerta no tenía dueño. Nadie lo notó. | *If it **had had** an owner, someone **would have noticed**.* |

**Contracciones (fundamentales, porque es como suena de verdad):**

| Completo | Contraído |
|---|---|
| If we had tested | *If we'**d** tested* |
| we would have caught | *we'**d have** caught* → habla: "we'd-a caught" |
| it would not have happened | *it **wouldn't have** happened* |

**Variantes en la cláusula principal:**

| Estructura | Matiz |
|---|---|
| *would have* + part. | consecuencia segura |
| *might / may have* + part. | consecuencia posible |
| *could have* + part. | consecuencia posible / capacidad |
| *wouldn't have* + part. | negación |

*If we'd tested it, we **might have caught** it.* (posible, más humilde que *would*)

**Inversión formal (sin *if*)** — muy usada en escritura técnica y de gran efecto retórico:

| Con *if* | Invertido |
|---|---|
| *If we had done that…* | ***Had we done that**…* |
| *If it hadn't been for the retry…* | ***Had it not been for** the retry…* |

> *"Had we done that, it would have shown up in the December diff"* suena notablemente más profesional que la versión con *if*, y es una de las estructuras que separan B2 de B1.

### 3 · Notice — condicionales mixtos

Mezclan un tiempo en la condición y otro en la consecuencia. Son extremadamente útiles porque la realidad casi nunca es simétrica.

| Tipo | Estructura | Significado | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Pasado → presente** | *If* + *had* + part., → *would* + inf | algo que no pasó afecta al presente | *If we'**d written** it down, we **wouldn't be** guessing now.* |
| **Presente → pasado** | *If* + pasado simple, → *would have* + part. | una condición permanente explica un hecho pasado | *If I **were** more familiar with the codebase, I'**d have spotted** it.* |

Estos dos son los que usan los hablantes avanzados en postmortems:
- *If the alert **had had** an owner, we **wouldn't be** in this meeting.* (pasado → presente)
- *If we **had** proper load testing, this **wouldn't have happened**.* (presente/general → pasado)

### 4 · Notice — *wish* y *if only*

Expresan deseo contrario a la realidad. La estructura es **la misma que el condicional**: retrocede un tiempo.

| Sobre | Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| **Presente** | *wish* + **pasado simple** | *I **wish** I **knew** why it failed.* (no lo sé) |
| **Presente (be)** | *wish* + **were** | *I **wish** it **were** simpler.* |
| **Pasado** | *wish* + **had** + part. | *I **wish** we'**d written** it down.* (no lo hicimos) |
| **Futuro / cambio de conducta** | *wish* + **would** | *I **wish** they'**d respond** faster.* (queja) |
| **Capacidad** | *wish* + **could** | *I **wish** I **could** help.* |

*if only* es idéntico pero más enfático: ***If only** we'd load-tested.*

> ⚠️ ❌ *I wish I would know.* ✅ *I wish I **knew**.*
> ⚠️ ❌ *I wish that we have documented it.* ✅ *I wish we **had documented** it.*
> ⚠️ *I wish* + *would* solo para cosas que otros hacen y te molestan, no para tus propios deseos: ❌ *I wish I would be taller.* ✅ *I wish I **were** taller.*

### 5 · Notice — crítica retrospectiva y expectativas incumplidas

| Estructura | Uso | Ejemplo |
|---|---|---|
| **should have** + part. | debimos y no lo hicimos (crítica) | *We **should have** caught this in review.* |
| **shouldn't have** + part. | no debimos y lo hicimos | *We **shouldn't have** deployed on a Friday.* |
| **could have** + part. | era posible (no necesariamente crítica) | *We **could have** used a feature flag.* |
| **needn't have** + part. | lo hicimos y no era necesario | *We **needn't have** rolled back — the fix was already live.* |
| **didn't need to** + inf | no era necesario y (probablemente) no lo hicimos | *We **didn't need to** roll back.* |
| **was / were supposed to** + inf | estaba previsto y no ocurrió | *It **was supposed to** be reverted in January.* |
| **was meant to** + inf | idem | *The lock **was meant to** prevent this.* |
| **ended up** + *-ing* | resultado no planeado | *We **ended up** rolling back.* |
| **failed to** + inf | no logramos (registro formal) | *We **failed to** detect it for three weeks.* |

**Vocabulario de retrospectiva:**

| Chunk | Uso |
|---|---|
| **In hindsight, …** | en retrospectiva |
| **With the benefit of hindsight, …** | idem, más formal |
| **Looking back, …** | |
| **What we missed was…** | |
| **The gap was that…** | |
| **Nobody owned it.** | nadie era responsable |
| **It fell through the cracks.** | se colaron entre las grietas |
| **It wasn't anyone's job.** | |
| **That's exactly the kind of gap we should be looking for.** | |
| **This isn't a "be more careful" problem.** | rechazar acciones no sistémicas |
| **The action item is systemic, not individual.** | |
| **We got away with it.** | nos salvamos |
| **It could have been much worse.** | |
| **Lessons learned** | |

> **Nota cultural sobre el postmortem sin culpables (*blameless postmortem*).** Es una convención muy arraigada en la ingeniería anglosajona: se asume que las personas actúan razonablemente con la información que tienen, y que un error individual es siempre síntoma de un fallo de sistema. Lingüísticamente esto se traduce en: **pasiva** en lugar de nombres, **we** en lugar de **you**, y acciones que corrigen procesos en lugar de personas.
> Compara: *"Maya raised the threshold and forgot to revert it"* vs *"The threshold was raised as a temporary measure, and there was no mechanism to ensure it was reverted."* El segundo es el registro esperado.

### 6 · Sound

**Foco:** *would have* / *had* contraídos son casi inaudibles y por eso los hispanohablantes no los producen nunca.

| Escrito | IPA real | Aproximación |
|---|---|---|
| If we'd tested | /ɪf wid ˈtestɪd/ | "if-wid-TES-tid" |
| we'd have caught | /wid əv ˈkɔːt/ | "wida-COT" |
| it wouldn't have happened | /ɪt ˈwʊdənt əv ˈhæpənd/ | "it-WOO-dn-ta-HA-pnd" |
| should have | /ˈʃʊdəv/ | "SHOO-da" |
| could have been | /ˈkʊdəv bɪn/ | "COO-da-bin" |
| Had we known | /(h)əd wi ˈnoʊn/ | "ad-wi-NOWN" |
| I wish we'd | /aɪ ˈwɪʃ wid/ | |

**Frase de entrenamiento (dila 10 veces):**
> *"If we'd load-tested it at production volumes, we'd have caught the leak, and none of this would have happened."*
> /ɪf wid ˈloʊd ˈtestɪd ɪt ət prəˈdʌkʃən ˈvɑːljumz, wid əv ˈkɔːt ðə ˈliːk, ən ˈnʌn əv ˈðɪs wʊdəv ˈhæpənd/

Tres contracciones seguidas. Si puedes decirla con fluidez, tienes el tercer condicional automatizado.

### 7 · Practice

**Ejercicio 19A — Tercer condicional.**
1. If we ____ (load-test) at production volumes, we ____ (catch) the leak.
2. If the threshold ____ (not be raised), the alert ____ (fire) twenty minutes earlier.
3. If the alert ____ (have) an owner, someone ____ (notice) it had been silenced.
4. If we ____ (write) it down as a ticket, it ____ (not fall) through the cracks.
5. If I ____ (know) about the backfill, I ____ (not start) the batch job.

**Ejercicio 19B — Condicionales mixtos. Identifica el tipo y completa.**
1. If we ____ (document) it, we ____ (not guess) now.
2. If I ____ (be) more familiar with the codebase, I ____ (spot) it in review.
3. If we ____ (have) proper load testing, this ____ (not happen).

**Ejercicio 19C — *wish* / *if only*.**
1. I wish I ____ (know) why it failed. *(no lo sé)*
2. I wish we ____ (write) it down. *(no lo hicimos)*
3. I wish it ____ (be) simpler. *(uso formal)*
4. I wish they ____ (respond) faster. *(queja sobre otros)*
5. If only we ____ (load-test) it first.

**Ejercicio 19D — *should have*, *needn't have*, *was supposed to*, *ended up*.**
1. We ____ caught this in review.
2. It ____ be reverted in January.
3. We ____ rolled back — the fix was already live.
4. We ____ rolling back anyway.
5. We ____ deployed on a Friday. *(no debimos)*

**Ejercicio 19E — Reescribe con inversión (sin *if*).**
1. *If we had done that, it would have shown up in the diff.*
2. *If it hadn't been for the retry logic, we'd have lost the payments.*
3. *If we had known about the backfill, we'd have waited.*

**Ejercicio 19F — Convierte a *blameless*.** Reescribe evitando señalar a la persona.
1. *Maya raised the threshold and forgot to revert it.*
2. *Tom deployed without running the tests.*
3. *You didn't check the dashboards.*
4. *The intern deleted the index.*

**Ejercicio 19G — Corrige.**
1. *If we would have tested it, we would have found it.*
2. *If we had tested it, we would find it.*
3. *I wish I would know.*
4. *We should of caught it.*
5. *I wish that we have documented it.*
6. *If only we would have known.*
7. *It was supposed to being reverted.*
8. *We didn't needed to roll back.*

**Ejercicio 19H — Traduce.**
1. Si hubiéramos hecho pruebas de carga a volúmenes de producción, habríamos detectado la fuga antes de la release.
2. Si no hubieran subido el umbral en diciembre, nos habrían avisado veinte minutos antes.
3. El impacto habría sido una fracción del que fue.
4. Si la alerta hubiera tenido un dueño, alguien se habría dado cuenta de que estaba silenciada.
5. No era el trabajo de nadie, y ese es exactamente el tipo de hueco que deberíamos buscar.
6. Ojalá lo hubiéramos apuntado como ticket.
7. Se suponía que se iba a revertir en enero; simplemente no ocurrió.
8. En retrospectiva, deberíamos haber tratado un cambio de umbral como un cambio de configuración.
9. De haberlo hecho así, habría aparecido en el diff de diciembre.
10. La acción no es "ser más cuidadosos", es "las alertas necesitan dueño".

### 8 · Challenge

**Reto 43.** Escribe la sección *"What could we have done differently"* de un postmortem real, en 10–12 frases. Requisitos: cuatro terceros condicionales, un condicional mixto, un *wish*, dos *should have*, un *was supposed to*, una inversión sin *if*, y **cero** nombres propios de personas.

**Reto 44.** Escribe la versión *"con culpables"* del mismo texto y compáralas lado a lado. Este ejercicio es incómodo a propósito: sirve para que veas exactamente qué mecanismos lingüísticos producen el efecto de neutralidad.

**Reto 45 (oral, 2 min).** Presenta la sección en voz alta. El tercer condicional en voz alta a velocidad natural es una de las cosas más difíciles del inglés para un hispanohablante. Grábate y mide cuántas contracciones produces.

### 9 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| *If we would have tested…* | If we **had** tested… | nunca *would* en la cláusula *if* |
| *If we had tested it, we would find it.* | …we **would have found** it. | ambas partes en pasado |
| *should of / could of / would of* | should **have** / could **have** | |
| *I wish I would know.* | I wish I **knew**. | |
| *I wish we have done it.* | I wish we **had** done it. | |
| *It was supposed to being reverted.* | …**to be** reverted. | |
| *We didn't needed to.* | We **didn't need to**. | |
| *If only we would have known.* | If only we **had** known. | |
| *I regret to not have said it.* | I regret **not saying / not having said** it. | |
| *It had to be worse.* | It **could have been** worse. | |

```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```


## UNIT 20 — Hedging, nuance and epistemic precision

**Nivel:** B2.2 · **Escenario:** escribes un RFC o un documento de diseño que será leído por gente con más autoridad que tú, y necesitas afirmar cosas de las que no estás totalmente seguro.
**Meta comunicativa:** calibrar exactamente el grado de certeza de cada afirmación.
**Gramática:** *hedging* avanzado · adverbios modales · *appear/seem/tend* · *it is likely that* · atribución de fuente.

### 1 · Por qué esto es nivel B2 y no antes

Un hablante A2 dice *"it will be faster"*. Un hablante B1 dice *"it should be faster"*. Un hablante B2 dice *"based on the benchmarks we ran, it appears to be roughly 30% faster at p99, though we haven't tested it under sustained load"*.

La diferencia no es vocabulario. Es que el B2 sabe expresar **exactamente cuánto sabe**, cómo lo sabe y qué queda sin saber. En ingeniería esto se llama honestidad epistémica y es una **competencia profesional**, no un adorno lingüístico. Un documento que exagera su certeza pierde credibilidad la primera vez que se equivoca.

### 2 · Input

> **RFC-0142: Migrating the event store to partitioned tables**
>
> **Summary.** We propose partitioning the `events` table by month. Based on a proof of concept run against a snapshot of production, this **appears to** reduce p99 read latency by around 40%, **although** the sample covered only two weeks of traffic and **may not** be representative of month-end load.
>
> **Confidence.** We are **fairly confident** about the read path: the query plans are **consistently** better across all the patterns we tested. We are **considerably less confident** about the write path — **it's possible that** the partition routing adds overhead we haven't measured, and there is **some evidence** in the Postgres mailing lists that this **can** become significant above roughly 50 partitions.
>
> **What we don't know.** We have **not** tested rollback under load. We are **assuming** — **but have not verified** — that pg_partman handles the retention window correctly with our current locking settings.
>
> **Recommendation.** We **would suggest** proceeding with a limited rollout to one tenant, **on the understanding that** we treat the write-path numbers as unvalidated until we have production data.

Observa que este texto es **más** persuasivo que uno rotundo, no menos. Declarar los límites de tu conocimiento genera confianza en lo que sí afirmas.

### 3 · Notice — el arsenal completo de *hedging*

**A) Verbos modales epistémicos** (revisión con matices finos):

| Modal | Certeza | Registro |
|---|---|---|
| *will* | ~100% | afirmación |
| *must* | ~95% (deducción) | |
| *should* | ~85% (expectativa) | |
| *ought to* | ~85% | más formal |
| *may / might / could* | ~50% | *may* es el más formal |
| *may well* | ~65% | *It may well be the cause.* |
| *might just* | ~40% | |
| *can* | posibilidad genérica | *This can become significant.* |

> ⚠️ Distinción fina: *may* / *might* = posibilidad **específica** (*it might be a race condition*). *can* = posibilidad **genérica o capacidad** (*race conditions can be hard to reproduce*). Confundirlos es un error muy común: ❌ *It can be the cause* (si te refieres a este caso concreto) ✅ *It **may** be the cause*.

**B) Verbos de apariencia y tendencia:**

| Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| *seem to* + inf | *It **seems to** happen only under load.* |
| *appear to* + inf (más formal) | *This **appears to** reduce latency by 40%.* |
| *tend to* + inf | *These tests **tend to** get flaky over time.* |
| *look like* + oración | *It **looks like** the count isn't reset.* |
| *There seems to be…* | *There **seems to be** a memory leak.* |
| *It would appear that…* | máxima cautela formal |

**C) Adverbios de probabilidad y su posición:**

| Adverbio | Certeza | Posición típica |
|---|---|---|
| *definitely / certainly* | alta | antes del verbo principal |
| *undoubtedly* | alta | |
| *almost certainly* | alta | |
| *probably / most likely* | media-alta | *It'll **probably** fail.* |
| *presumably* | inferencia | *Presumably they tested it.* |
| *apparently* | según lo que parece / según dicen | |
| *seemingly* | | |
| *arguably* | defendible | *Arguably the cleaner approach.* |
| *possibly / perhaps / maybe* | media-baja | |
| *conceivably* | baja | |
| *In principle / In theory* | teórico | |
| *In practice* | contraste con teoría | |

> **Nota de posición:** los adverbios de probabilidad van **antes** del verbo principal pero **después** del auxiliar: *It **will probably** fail.* / *It **has probably** failed.* / *It **is probably** the cause.*
> ❌ *Probably it will fail* (posible pero suena a traducción) ✅ *It'll probably fail.*

**D) Estructuras impersonales de probabilidad:**

| Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| *It is likely / unlikely that…* | *It's **unlikely that** this is the root cause.* |
| *X is likely to* + inf | *This is **likely to** increase load.* |
| *There is a chance / risk that…* | |
| *The odds are that…* | informal |
| *It's not inconceivable that…* | doble negación, muy cauto |
| *It remains to be seen whether…* | |
| *It's an open question whether…* | |

**E) Cuantificadores cautelosos:**

| Preciso pero cauto | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| *roughly / approximately / around / in the region of* | *around 40%* |
| *up to* | *up to two minutes* |
| *at least / at most* | |
| *on the order of* | *on the order of 10ms* |
| *broadly / by and large* | *broadly in line with expectations* |
| *in most cases / typically* | |
| *in some cases* | |
| *to some extent* | |
| *marginally* | |

**F) Atribución de fuente (esencial en un RFC):**

| Estructura | Función |
|---|---|
| *Based on X, …* | declarar la evidencia |
| *According to X, …* | atribuir a fuente externa |
| *In my experience, …* | atribuir a experiencia propia |
| *As far as I can tell, …* | limitar a tu conocimiento |
| *To the best of my knowledge, …* | idem, más formal |
| *From what I've seen, …* | |
| *There is some evidence that…* | evidencia parcial |
| *Anecdotally, …* | evidencia no sistemática |
| *We are assuming — but have not verified — that…* | asunción explícita |
| *I haven't verified this, but…* | |

**G) Declarar límites explícitamente** (el rasgo más característico del B2 técnico):

| Chunk |
|---|
| *We have not tested X.* |
| *This is based on a limited sample.* |
| *The sample may not be representative of Y.* |
| *This assumes Z, which we haven't validated.* |
| *I'd treat these numbers as indicative rather than definitive.* |
| *Take this with a pinch of salt.* [UK] / *grain of salt* [US] |
| *Caveat: …* |
| *One thing I'm not sure about is…* |
| *The main unknown is…* |
| *Where I'd want more data is…* |

### 4 · Notice — cuándo NO hacer *hedging*

Un exceso de suavización es tan malo como su ausencia. Produce textos que no dicen nada y que se leen como evasión. Hay contextos donde debes ser rotundo:

| Sé rotundo cuando… | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| Es un hecho verificado | *The pool was exhausted at 02:14. That's in the logs.* |
| Es una obligación de seguridad | *Secrets must never be committed to the repo.* |
| Es tu recomendación final | *My recommendation is to proceed with option B.* |
| Alguien pide una decisión, no un análisis | *Yes. Let's do it.* |
| Estás informando de un riesgo grave | *This will lose data if the node fails mid-write.* |

> **La regla:** haz *hedging* sobre **predicciones, interpretaciones y estimaciones**. No lo hagas sobre **hechos, obligaciones y decisiones**. Confundir ambas categorías es lo que hace que un texto suene débil.

**Comparación:**

| Débil (hedging mal aplicado) | Correcto |
|---|---|
| *It might possibly be the case that the pool was perhaps exhausted.* | *The pool was exhausted.* (es un hecho) |
| *Maybe we should perhaps consider possibly not committing secrets.* | *Secrets must never be committed.* |
| *I sort of think option B could maybe be better?* | *I'd recommend option B, mainly because of the rollback story.* |

### 5 · Sound

**Foco:** los *hedges* son **átonos** y rápidos; el contenido lleva el acento. Un hispanohablante que acentúa el *hedge* suena dubitativo, no cauto.

| Frase | Sílabas fuertes |
|---|---|
| *It appears to reduce latency by about forty percent.* | re-**DUCE**, **LA**-ten-cy, **FOR**-ty |
| *We're fairly confident about the read path.* | **FAIR**-ly, **CON**-fi-dent, **READ** path |
| *It may well be the cause.* | **MAY WELL** (aquí sí es tónico), **CAUSE** |

**Práctica:** lee el Input de la sección 2 en voz alta, acentuando solo los sustantivos y verbos de contenido y pasando rápido por los *hedges*. Debe sonar seguro, no titubeante. La cautela está en las palabras, no en la voz.

### 6 · Practice

**Ejercicio 20A — Calibra la certeza.** Reescribe cada afirmación al grado indicado.
1. *It's a race condition.* → 50%
2. *It's a race condition.* → 95% deducción
3. *It's a race condition.* → 85% expectativa
4. *This will reduce latency by 40%.* → basado en evidencia limitada
5. *This is the root cause.* → improbable

**Ejercicio 20B — Coloca el adverbio.**
1. (probably) *It will fail under load.*
2. (arguably) *That's the cleaner approach.*
3. (presumably) *They tested it before merging.*
4. (almost certainly) *The leak has been there since 4.12.*
5. (apparently) *The platform team has already deprecated it.*

**Ejercicio 20C — *may*, *might*, *can* o *could*.**
1. Race conditions ____ be very hard to reproduce. *(genérico)*
2. It ____ be a race condition — I'm about 50/50 on it. *(específico)*
3. This ____ well be the cause. *(65%)*
4. Above 50 partitions, the overhead ____ become significant. *(posibilidad genérica)*
5. ____ you take a look when you get a chance? *(petición)*

**Ejercicio 20D — Añade la declaración de límite adecuada.**
1. *This reduces p99 latency by 40%.* → añade que la muestra fue de dos semanas.
2. *pg_partman handles the retention window correctly.* → añade que es una asunción no verificada.
3. *The write path is fine.* → añade que no lo has medido.

**Ejercicio 20E — Corrige el *hedging* mal aplicado.**
1. *It might possibly be the case that the pool was perhaps exhausted at 02:14.*
2. *Maybe we should perhaps consider not committing secrets.*
3. *I sort of think option B could maybe be better?*
4. *Probably it will fail.*
5. *It can be the cause of this outage.*
6. *In my opinion I think that possibly…*

**Ejercicio 20F — Traduce (mantén el grado exacto de certeza).**
1. Basándonos en una prueba de concepto contra una instantánea de producción, esto parece reducir la latencia de lectura p99 en torno al 40%.
2. Aunque la muestra cubría solo dos semanas de tráfico y puede no ser representativa de la carga de fin de mes.
3. Estamos bastante seguros del camino de lectura.
4. Estamos considerablemente menos seguros del camino de escritura.
5. Es posible que el enrutado de particiones añada una sobrecarga que no hemos medido.
6. Hay algunas indicaciones en las listas de correo de Postgres de que esto puede volverse significativo.
7. No hemos probado la reversión bajo carga.
8. Asumimos, pero no hemos verificado, que pg_partman gestiona correctamente la ventana de retención.
9. Yo trataría estas cifras como indicativas y no como definitivas.
10. Recomendaríamos proceder con un despliegue limitado a un solo tenant.

### 7 · Challenge

**Reto 46.** Escribe un RFC corto (250–350 palabras) sobre una propuesta técnica real, con las secciones: *Summary*, *Confidence*, *What we don't know*, *Recommendation*. Requisitos: tres grados distintos de certeza claramente diferenciados, dos atribuciones de fuente, tres declaraciones explícitas de límite, una recomendación final **sin** hedging.

**Reto 47 (autoauditoría).** Coge el último documento técnico que hayas escrito en inglés. Subraya cada afirmación y clasifícala: hecho verificado / interpretación / predicción / asunción. Comprueba si el grado de certeza del lenguaje corresponde a la categoría. Casi todo el mundo descubre que afirma predicciones como si fueran hechos.

**Reto 48 (oral).** Responde en voz alta a *"Are you sure this will work?"* con tres versiones: una honesta y cauta, una honesta y firme, y una que distinga explícitamente qué sabes de qué asumes.

### 8 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto |
|---|---|
| *Probably it will fail.* | It'll **probably** fail. |
| *It can be the cause.* (caso concreto) | It **may/might** be the cause. |
| *I think that maybe possibly…* | I suspect… / My sense is… |
| *In my opinion I think…* | redundante: elige una |
| *It's sure that…* | It's **certain** that… / **Certainly**, … |
| *I am agree that it's likely.* | I **agree** that… |
| *Is possible that…* | **It's** possible that… |
| *Depends of the load.* | **It depends on** the load. |
| *More or less 40%* | **Around / roughly** 40% |
| *Actually the numbers are…* | **Currently / In fact** the numbers are… |

## UNIT 21 — Emphasis and persuasion: cleft sentences, inversion and fronting

**Nivel:** B2.3 · **Escenario:** un documento de diseño donde necesitas que el lector retenga **una** idea, o una discusión donde necesitas redirigir la atención.
**Meta comunicativa:** controlar dónde cae el foco de la información.
**Gramática:** *cleft sentences* (*it is… that*, *what… is*) · inversión enfática · *fronting* · *do* enfático.

### 1 · Por qué el orden importa

El inglés tiene un orden de palabras rígido, pero dispone de **estructuras de reordenamiento** que permiten mover el foco. Un hablante B1 escribe frases correctas en orden neutro. Un hablante B2 elige el orden para que el lector se lleve la idea correcta.

Compara estas cuatro frases, todas correctas y con el mismo contenido:

| Frase | Foco |
|---|---|
| *The retry logic is the problem.* | neutro |
| ***It's the retry logic that** is the problem.* | contraste: no es otra cosa, es esto |
| ***What's causing the problem is** the retry logic.* | suspense: primero el problema, después la causa |
| ***The retry logic — that's** what's causing the problem.* | énfasis coloquial fuerte |

### 2 · Input

> **Design doc — extract**
>
> **What we're proposing is not a rewrite.** It's a strangler pattern: we route new traffic through the new service and leave the old one running until it's dead.
>
> **It's the coupling between billing and notifications that has caused most of our incidents this year** — not the database, not the deploy process, and not the team's velocity. Four of the last six incidents trace back to that one boundary.
>
> **What surprised us most when we mapped it out was how little of the code actually needs to move.** Roughly 8% of the billing module touches notification state. The rest can stay where it is.
>
> **Only after we've split that boundary should we even discuss whether the rest of the monolith needs breaking up.** Doing both at once is how migrations die.
>
> **We do accept** that this leaves us with two deployment units for longer than anyone would like. **What we don't accept** is that the alternative — a big-bang rewrite — has ever worked at this company.

### 3 · Notice — *cleft sentences* con *it*

**Estructura:** *It is / was* + **elemento enfatizado** + *that / who* + resto.

Sirve para **contrastar**: señalar que es X y no otra cosa.

| Neutro | Con *it*-cleft |
|---|---|
| *The coupling caused the incidents.* | ***It was the coupling that** caused the incidents.* |
| *Maya found the leak.* | ***It was Maya who** found the leak.* |
| *We noticed it in December.* | ***It wasn't until December that** we noticed it.* |
| *We need better tests, not more monitoring.* | ***It's better tests that** we need, not more monitoring.* |

**Variantes muy útiles:**

| Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| *It was only when… that…* | *It was only when we ran it at scale that the leak appeared.* |
| *It wasn't until… that…* | *It wasn't until December that anyone noticed.* |
| *It's not that X, it's that Y.* | *It's not that the code is slow — it's that we call it 400 times per request.* |
| *It's X, not Y, that…* | *It's the boundary, not the database, that's the problem.* |

> *"It's not that X, it's that Y"* es una de las estructuras más útiles del inglés técnico: sirve para corregir el diagnóstico de otra persona sin contradecirla frontalmente.

### 4 · Notice — *cleft sentences* con *what*

**Estructura:** *What* + oración + *is / was* + elemento enfatizado.

Sirve para crear **suspense**: pones el problema primero y la respuesta al final, donde el lector la retiene.

| Neutro | Con *what*-cleft |
|---|---|
| *The retry logic is causing the problem.* | ***What's causing the problem is** the retry logic.* |
| *We need a read model.* | ***What we need is** a read model.* |
| *We're proposing a strangler pattern.* | ***What we're proposing is** a strangler pattern.* |
| *Very little code needs to move.* | ***What surprised us was** how little code needs to move.* |

**Variantes:**

| Estructura | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| *What matters is…* | *What matters is the failure mode, not the happy path.* |
| *What I'd do is…* | *What I'd do is add a feature flag first.* |
| *What worries me is…* | *What worries me is the blast radius.* |
| *What we don't know is…* | |
| *All (that) we need is…* | *All we need is one more week.* |
| *The reason (why) X is that…* | *The reason it's slow is that we're not batching.* |
| *The thing is, …* | informal, muy usado |
| *The problem with X is that…* | |

> ⚠️ El verbo va en **singular** después de *what*: *What we need **is** two things.* (aunque siga un plural — es aceptable y frecuente; también se oye *are*).

### 5 · Notice — inversión enfática

Cuando una expresión negativa o restrictiva va al **inicio** de la frase, el inglés **invierte** sujeto y auxiliar, como en una pregunta. Es formal y de gran impacto.

| Expresión inicial | Ejemplo |
|---|---|
| **Not only… but also** | ***Not only did** it fail, **but it also** took the notification service down.* |
| **Never / Rarely / Seldom** | ***Never have I seen** a stack trace that deep.* |
| **Only after / Only when / Only if** | ***Only after** we've split that boundary **should we** discuss the rest.* |
| **Under no circumstances** | ***Under no circumstances should** secrets be committed.* |
| **At no point** | ***At no point did** we consider a big-bang rewrite.* |
| **Little** | ***Little did we know** it had been there for three weeks.* |
| **No sooner… than** | ***No sooner had we deployed than** the alerts fired.* |
| **Hardly / Scarcely** | ***Hardly had it gone live when** the first report came in.* |
| **Had / Were / Should** (condicional) | ***Had we tested it**, we'd have caught it.* |
| **So… that** | ***So severe was** the impact **that** we froze deploys for a week.* |

**Mecánica de la inversión:** si hay auxiliar, se adelanta; si no hay, se añade *do/does/did*.
*It failed.* → *Not only **did it** fail…*
*We have seen…* → *Never **have we** seen…*
*We should discuss…* → *Only then **should we** discuss…*

> Uso profesional: la inversión es **potente pero cara**. Una por documento tiene mucho efecto; cinco suenan a discurso político. Resérvala para la frase que quieres que se recuerde.

### 6 · Notice — *fronting* (adelantar elementos)

Mover un elemento al principio sin invertir, para enlazar con lo anterior o para crear contraste.

| Neutro | Con *fronting* |
|---|---|
| *I completely agree with that part.* | ***That part** I completely agree with.* |
| *We can fix the rest later.* | ***The rest** we can fix later.* |
| *I have no idea why it works.* | ***Why it works**, I have no idea.* |
| *The code is fine; the boundary is the problem.* | ***The code**, fine. **The boundary**, that's the problem.* |

Y con adverbiales, muy común en escritura técnica:
*In practice, this rarely happens.* · *Under load, the behaviour changes completely.* · *At p99, the difference disappears.*

### 7 · Notice — *do* enfático

Añadir *do / does / did* a una afirmativa la refuerza. En español equivaldría a "sí que…".

| Neutro | Enfático |
|---|---|
| *We accept that this leaves two deploy units.* | *We **do** accept that…* |
| *It works, but only under low load.* | *It **does** work, but only under low load.* |
| *I told you.* | *I **did** tell you.* |
| *Please look at this.* | ***Do** take a look at this.* (formal, cortés) |

Uso típico: **conceder** algo antes de discrepar. *"I **do** see the appeal of a rewrite. What I don't see is how we'd ship anything for six months."* Es una de las estructuras más eficaces de la discusión profesional.

### 8 · Sound

**Foco:** en estas estructuras, el elemento enfatizado lleva el **pico tonal**. Si lo lees plano, la estructura se desperdicia.

| Frase | Pico |
|---|---|
| *It was the **COU**pling that caused it.* | en *coupling* |
| *What we need is a **READ** model.* | en *read model* |
| *Not only **DID** it fail…* | en *did* |
| *We **DO** accept that…* | en *do* |
| *It's not that it's **SLOW** — it's that we call it **FOUR HUNDRED** times.* | doble pico contrastivo |

**Práctica:** lee el Input de la sección 2 en voz alta exagerando los picos tonales. Después lee la versión neutra ("The coupling caused most of our incidents"). Nota cuánto se pierde.

### 9 · Practice

**Ejercicio 21A — Reescribe con *it*-cleft.**
1. *The coupling caused most of our incidents.*
2. *Maya found the leak.*
3. *We only noticed it in December.*
4. *We need better tests, not more dashboards.*

**Ejercicio 21B — Reescribe con *what*-cleft.**
1. *The retry logic is causing the problem.*
2. *We need a separate read model.*
3. *We're proposing a strangler pattern, not a rewrite.*
4. *The blast radius worries me.*
5. *Very little of the code actually needs to move.* *(empieza con "What surprised us…")*

**Ejercicio 21C — Invierte.**
1. *It didn't only fail; it also took down notifications.* *(Not only…)*
2. *I have never seen a stack trace that deep.* *(Never…)*
3. *We should only discuss the rest after we've split the boundary.* *(Only after…)*
4. *Secrets should never be committed under any circumstances.* *(Under no circumstances…)*
5. *We didn't know it had been there for three weeks.* *(Little…)*
6. *If we had tested it, we'd have caught it.* *(Had…)*

**Ejercicio 21D — Añade *do* enfático.**
1. *We accept that this leaves two deployment units.*
2. *It works, but only under low load.*
3. *I see the appeal of a rewrite.*

**Ejercicio 21E — Corrige.**
1. *It is the coupling what caused the incidents.*
2. *What we need are a read model.* *(aceptable pero elige la forma estándar)*
3. *Not only it failed, but also it took down notifications.*
4. *Never I have seen that.*
5. *Only after we split it, we should discuss the rest.*
6. *Is the boundary that is the problem.*
7. *What I do is I would add a feature flag.*
8. *Under no circumstances secrets should be committed.*

**Ejercicio 21F — Traduce manteniendo el énfasis.**
1. Lo que proponemos no es una reescritura.
2. Es el acoplamiento entre facturación y notificaciones lo que ha causado la mayoría de nuestros incidentes este año.
3. Lo que más nos sorprendió al mapearlo fue lo poco código que hay que mover realmente.
4. Solo después de separar esa frontera deberíamos plantearnos si hay que romper el resto del monolito.
5. Sí aceptamos que esto nos deja con dos unidades de despliegue más tiempo del que a nadie le gustaría.
6. Lo que no aceptamos es que la alternativa haya funcionado alguna vez en esta empresa.
7. No es que el código sea lento; es que lo llamamos cuatrocientas veces por petición.
8. Nunca había visto una traza de pila tan profunda.

### 10 · Challenge

**Reto 49.** Escribe la sección de apertura de un documento de diseño (200–250 palabras) defendiendo una decisión técnica real. Requisitos: un *it*-cleft, dos *what*-clefts, una inversión, un *do* enfático, un *"It's not that X, it's that Y"*, y una única idea que el lector deba recordar al terminar.

**Reto 50.** Coge un párrafo neutro que hayas escrito y reescríbelo tres veces desplazando el foco a tres elementos distintos. Observa cómo cambia lo que el lector se lleva.

**Reto 51 (oral, 2 min).** Presenta tu argumento en voz alta con los picos tonales marcados. Grábate. Escucha si el énfasis se oye o si tu melodía es plana; en ese caso las estructuras no sirven de nada.

### 11 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| *It is the coupling **what** caused it.* | …**that** caused it. | el español usa "lo que"; el inglés *that* |
| *Is the boundary that…* | **It's** the boundary that… | sujeto obligatorio |
| *Not only it failed…* | Not only **did it** fail… | inversión obligatoria |
| *Never I have seen…* | Never **have I** seen… | |
| *Only after we split it, we should…* | Only after we split it **should we**… | |
| *What I do is I add a flag.* | What I'd do is **add** a flag. | infinitivo sin *to* tras *is* |
| *The thing is that is complex.* | The thing is **it's** complex. | |
| *For that reason is important.* | For that reason **it's** important. | |
| *Is not that it's slow.* | **It's** not that it's slow. | |
| *Also it took down notifications.* | **It also** took down notifications. | posición de *also* |

## UNIT 22 — The technical interview

**Nivel:** B2.3 · **Escenario:** una entrevista de 4 rondas en inglés: behavioural, live coding, system design y *"do you have any questions for us?"*.
**Meta comunicativa:** narrar tu experiencia con estructura, pensar en voz alta mientras programas, y diseñar un sistema hablando.
**Lengua:** método STAR · pensamiento en voz alta · lenguaje de diseño de sistemas · preguntas al entrevistador.

> **Por qué esta unidad es distinta.** En una entrevista técnica no te evalúan la gramática: te evalúan si **saben lo que estás pensando**. Un ingeniero excelente que se queda en silencio suspende. Un ingeniero medio que narra su razonamiento con claridad aprueba. Todo lo de esta unidad va dirigido a eso: convertir tu pensamiento en habla continua.

### 1 · Input — behavioural round

> **INTERVIEWER:** Tell me about a time you disagreed with a technical decision.
>
> **YOU:** Sure. **So, for context**, about a year ago I was on a team of five maintaining the payments service. **The situation was that** we'd been asked to add a second payment provider, and the tech lead's plan was to add a conditional branch in the existing integration — basically an `if provider == 'stripe'` running through the whole module.
>
> **My concern was that** we already had four different responsibilities in that file, and adding a second provider that way would have made it effectively untestable. **So what I did was** — rather than argue about it in the review — I spent an afternoon writing a small proof of concept with an adapter interface, and I put both versions side by side in the PR description, with the test count for each.
>
> **The outcome was that** we went with the adapter. It took about two days longer up front. **What I took away from it** is that a working proof of concept moves a conversation much faster than an argument does — and honestly, that I should have raised it earlier, before the plan was already written down.
>
> **INTERVIEWER:** What would you have done differently?
>
> **YOU:** **In hindsight**, I'd have asked to see the plan before it was finalised. I'd assumed someone else was reviewing the design, and **it turned out** nobody was. That's a gap I now check for explicitly.

### 2 · Notice — el método STAR

El estándar anglosajón para responder preguntas de comportamiento. Cuatro bloques, en orden, sin desviarse.

| Bloque | Contenido | Frases de entrada | Duración |
|---|---|---|---|
| **S**ituation | contexto mínimo necesario | *For context, …* / *The situation was that…* / *At the time, I was…* | 15–20 s |
| **T**ask | tu responsabilidad concreta | *My job was to…* / *I was asked to…* / *My concern was that…* | 10–15 s |
| **A**ction | **qué hiciste tú** (60% del tiempo) | *So what I did was…* / *I started by…* / *Rather than X, I…* | 60–90 s |
| **R**esult | resultado + aprendizaje | *The outcome was that…* / *What I took away from it is…* | 20–30 s |

> **Los tres errores mortales del STAR:**
> 1. **Demasiada S.** Cinco minutos de contexto y treinta segundos de acción. Recorta el contexto a lo imprescindible.
> 2. ***We* en lugar de *I*.** Los hispanohablantes decimos *"we decided", "we implemented"* por modestia cultural. El entrevistador necesita saber **qué hiciste tú**. Usa *I* en la sección Action y *we* solo para el equipo. Di *"the team decided X; my part was Y"*.
> 3. **Sin resultado medible ni aprendizaje.** Cierra siempre con un número o un cambio de conducta.

**Banco de preguntas de comportamiento (prepara una historia para cada una):**

| Pregunta | Lo que evalúan |
|---|---|
| *Tell me about a time you disagreed with a technical decision.* | conflicto, influencia sin autoridad |
| *Tell me about a project you're proud of.* | motivación, propiedad |
| *Tell me about a time you made a mistake in production.* | honestidad, aprendizaje |
| *Tell me about a time you had to deliver under a tight deadline.* | trade-offs, priorización |
| *Tell me about giving difficult feedback.* | madurez |
| *How do you handle a code review where you disagree with the reviewer?* | ego |
| *Tell me about something you learned recently.* | curiosidad |
| *Describe the hardest bug you've debugged.* | rigor técnico |
| *Tell me about working with a difficult colleague.* | trampa: nunca hables mal de nadie |
| *Why are you leaving your current role?* | trampa: enmarca en positivo |

**Chunks para hablar de logros sin sonar arrogante ni minimizar:**

| Chunk |
|---|
| *I led the migration of…* |
| *I was responsible for…* |
| *I owned the… end to end.* |
| *I drove the decision to…* |
| *I took the initiative to…* |
| *My contribution was…* |
| *The part I was directly responsible for was…* |
| *I can't take credit for the design, but I did implement…* |
| *We cut p99 latency from 800ms to 120ms.* |
| *That reduced our on-call pages by about 70%.* |
| *It's now handling roughly 4,000 requests a second.* |

> ⚠️ Trampa cultural inversa: en inglés profesional **no se espera modestia**. *"I just helped a bit with…"* se lee literalmente como que hiciste poco. Di lo que hiciste, con datos, en tono neutro. Eso no es arrogancia: es información.

### 3 · Notice — pensar en voz alta (*live coding*)

Aquí necesitas producir habla continua mientras tu atención está en el problema. La solución es memorizar tanto que no requiera atención.

**A) Clarificar el problema antes de escribir:**

| Chunk |
|---|
| *Before I start, can I clarify a couple of things?* |
| *Just to make sure I've understood — you want…?* |
| *Can I assume the input is always valid?* |
| *What should happen if the list is empty?* |
| *Are we optimising for time or for memory here?* |
| *Roughly what size input are we expecting?* |
| *Should I handle duplicates, or can I assume they're unique?* |
| *Let me restate the problem to check I've got it.* |
| *Can I use built-in libraries, or do you want it from scratch?* |
| *Would you like me to write tests as well?* |

**B) Anunciar tu plan:**

| Chunk |
|---|
| *My first thought is a brute-force approach, and then I'll look at optimising.* |
| *I'll start with the naive solution just to get something working.* |
| *Let me talk through the approach before I write any code.* |
| *There are two ways I can see doing this. Let me weigh them up.* |
| *The trade-off between them is…* |
| *I'm going to go with the hash-map approach, mainly because…* |

**C) Narrar mientras escribes:**

| Chunk |
|---|
| *So I'll iterate over the array and…* |
| *I'm going to keep a running total here.* |
| *I'll use a set so lookups are constant time.* |
| *Let me pull this out into a helper function.* |
| *I'll come back to the edge cases in a moment.* |
| *I'm going to name this something better later.* |
| *Bear with me a second while I think.* |
| *Let me just sanity-check this bit.* |

**D) Cuando te atascas** (lo más importante — el silencio es lo que suspende):

| Chunk |
|---|
| *Let me think out loud for a second.* |
| *I'm not immediately seeing the trick here, so let me work through a small example.* |
| *Let me try a concrete case: if the input is [1, 2, 3]…* |
| *I've got a feeling there's a cleaner way, but let me get this working first.* |
| *I'm stuck on the off-by-one here. Let me trace through it.* |
| *Could I get a nudge on this bit?* |
| *Am I on the right track?* |
| *Let me back up — I don't think that approach works, because…* |
| *Actually, scrap that. Here's a better way.* |

**E) Complejidad y cierre:**

| Chunk |
|---|
| *That's O(n) time and O(n) space.* → /oʊ əv en/ |
| *The dominant cost is the sort, so it's O(n log n).* → "oh of en log en" |
| *We could bring that down to O(n) if we…* |
| *That's linear / quadratic / logarithmic / constant time.* |
| *There's an edge case I haven't handled: what if…* |
| *If I had more time, I'd add tests for…* |
| *I'd want to profile this before assuming it's fast enough.* |

### 4 · Notice — *system design round*

Es la ronda que más inglés exige, porque hablas 40 minutos casi sin parar. Estructura estándar en cinco fases, con lenguaje fijo para cada una:

| Fase | Chunks de apertura |
|---|---|
| **1. Requisitos** | *Let's start with requirements. Who are the users, and what are the core flows?* / *Are we designing for read-heavy or write-heavy traffic?* / *What scale are we targeting?* / *Is there a latency budget?* / *What's in scope and what can I leave out?* |
| **2. Estimaciones** | *Let's do some rough numbers.* / *If we assume a million daily active users, and each one does ten reads…* / *That gives us roughly 100 requests a second average, with peaks maybe five times that.* / *Back-of-the-envelope, we're looking at about 2 TB a year.* |
| **3. Diseño de alto nivel** | *Let me sketch the high-level components first, then drill into each one.* / *At a high level, we've got a load balancer, a stateless API layer, and a datastore.* / *I'll keep it simple to begin with and add complexity where it's needed.* |
| **4. Profundizar** | *Let's zoom in on the write path.* / *The interesting problem here is…* / *There are a few ways to shard this. The trade-off is…* / *I'd put a cache in front of that, with a read-through policy.* |
| **5. Fallos y trade-offs** | *Let's talk about what happens when things break.* / *If that node goes down, we lose…* / *This gives us eventual consistency, which is fine for the feed but not for balances.* / *The main bottleneck I'd expect is…* / *If I had to pick one thing to improve, it'd be…* |

**Vocabulario de diseño imprescindible (con IPA de los que se pronuncian mal):**

| Término | IPA | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| latency | /ˈleɪtənsi/ | "LEI-ten-si", no "la-TEN-cia" |
| throughput | /ˈθruːpʊt/ | /θ/ inicial |
| availability | /əˌveɪləˈbɪləti/ | acento en *bil* |
| consistency | /kənˈsɪstənsi/ | |
| idempotent | /aɪˈdempətənt/ | "ai-DEM-po-tent" |
| cache | /kæʃ/ | = *cash* |
| queue | /kjuː/ | "kiu" |
| shard / sharding | /ʃɑːrd/ | |
| replica | /ˈreplɪkə/ | acento en *rep* |
| quorum | /ˈkwɔːrəm/ | |
| eventual consistency | /ɪˈventʃuəl/ | ⚠️ *eventual* = "final", no "posible" |
| bottleneck | /ˈbɑːtlnek/ | |
| back-of-the-envelope | | "cálculo a ojo" |
| single point of failure | | |
| blast radius | | alcance del daño |
| graceful degradation | /ˈɡreɪsfəl/ | |
| circuit breaker | /ˈsɜːrkɪt/ | |
| write-ahead log | | |
| fan-out | | |
| hot partition | | |
| tail latency | | latencia de cola (p99) |
| trade-off | | ⚠️ nunca *"trade of"* |

### 5 · Notice — tus preguntas al entrevistador

Se evalúa. Preguntas genéricas restan. Estas son concretas y revelan seniority:

| Pregunta | Qué señala |
|---|---|
| *What does the on-call rotation look like in practice?* | realismo operacional |
| *How do changes get from a laptop to production? Walk me through it.* | interés por el proceso |
| *How long does CI take, and how often is the pipeline red?* | rigor |
| *How are technical decisions made when people disagree?* | madurez |
| *What's the last piece of tech debt the team actually paid down?* | detecta si dicen "sí, invertimos en calidad" sin hacerlo |
| *What would you want me to have achieved by the end of month three?* | orientación a resultados |
| *What's the hardest problem the team is facing right now?* | |
| *How much of the week is meetings, honestly?* | |
| *What happened to the last person in this role?* | directa pero legítima |
| *Is there anything about my background that gives you pause?* | permite rebatir objeciones antes de irte |

Y para cerrar: *"Thanks — that was useful. What are the next steps, and what's the timeline?"*

### 6 · Sound

**Foco:** las tres cosas que más se malinterpretan al hablar de técnica.

**a) Deletrear y leer símbolos** — pruébalos todos en voz alta:

| Símbolo | Se dice |
|---|---|
| `_` | *underscore* |
| `-` | *dash* / *hyphen* |
| `/` | *slash* / *forward slash* |
| `\` | *backslash* |
| `[]` | *square brackets* |
| `{}` | *curly braces* / *curly brackets* |
| `()` | *parentheses* /pəˈrenθəsiːz/ · *brackets* [UK] |
| `<>` | *angle brackets* |
| `.` | *dot* (en URLs y ficheros) · *full stop*/*period* (en prosa) |
| `:` | *colon* /ˈkoʊlən/ |
| `;` | *semicolon* |
| `@` | *at* |
| `#` | *hash* [UK] · *pound*/*number sign* [US] |
| `*` | *asterisk* /ˈæstərɪsk/ |
| `!` | *bang* / *exclamation mark* |
| `?` | *question mark* |
| `&` | *ampersand* |
| `^` | *caret* /ˈkærət/ |
| `~` | *tilde* /ˈtɪldə/ |
| `%` | *percent* |
| `\|` | *pipe* |
| `=>` | *fat arrow* |
| `->` | *arrow* |
| `::` | *double colon* |
| `!=` | *not equal(s)* |
| `===` | *triple equals* |

**b) Letras que los hispanohablantes deletrean mal:**

| Letra | IPA | Confusión típica |
|---|---|---|
| **A** | /eɪ/ | ⚠️ no /a/ |
| **E** | /iː/ | ⚠️ confundida con **I** |
| **I** | /aɪ/ | ⚠️ confundida con **E** |
| **G** | /dʒiː/ | ⚠️ confundida con **J** |
| **J** | /dʒeɪ/ | |
| **H** | /eɪtʃ/ | |
| **W** | /ˈdʌbəljuː/ | |
| **Y** | /waɪ/ | |
| **Z** | /ziː/ [US] · /zed/ [UK] | |
| **R** | /ɑːr/ | |

**c) Números:**

| Escrito | Se dice |
|---|---|
| 0 | *zero* · *oh* (en secuencias) · *nil* [UK, marcadores] |
| 1,000 | *a thousand* |
| 100k | *a hundred K* / *a hundred thousand* |
| 1M | *one million* / *one mil* |
| 0.5 | *nought point five* [UK] / *zero point five* / *point five* |
| 1.5x | *one point five times* / *one and a half times* |
| 2/3 | *two thirds* |
| 40% | *forty percent* |
| p99 | *p ninety-nine* |
| 200ms | *two hundred milliseconds* / *two hundred mils* |
| 3.14 | *three point one four* |
| v2.1.3 | *version two point one point three* |
| 2026 | *twenty twenty-six* |
| $85k | *eighty-five K* / *eighty-five thousand* |

### 7 · Practice

**Ejercicio 22A — Etiqueta las fases STAR.** Marca en el Input de la sección 1 dónde empieza cada bloque S/T/A/R y calcula qué proporción ocupa la Action.

**Ejercicio 22B — Reescribe pasando de *we* a *I*** (sin sonar arrogante).
1. *We decided to use an adapter interface.*
2. *We reduced latency a lot.*
3. *We fixed the bug and shipped it.*
4. *We migrated the database.*

**Ejercicio 22C — Reformula las respuestas débiles.**
1. *I just helped a bit with the migration.*
2. *It was a team effort, nothing special.*
3. *I don't know, we made it faster I think.*
4. *My English is not very good, sorry.*

**Ejercicio 22D — Lee en voz alta.**
1. `user_id` → `users/{id}/settings`
2. `if (x !== y) { return null; }`
3. *That's O(n log n) time and O(1) space.*
4. `https://api.example.com/v2.1/orders?status=paid&limit=50`
5. *p99 went from 850ms to 120ms — about a 7x improvement.*
6. `SELECT * FROM events WHERE created_at > NOW() - INTERVAL '1 day';`

**Ejercicio 22E — Elige el chunk para cada situación.**
1. Llevas veinte segundos en silencio y no ves la solución.
2. Te das cuenta de que tu enfoque no funciona a medio camino.
3. Quieres saber si el input puede estar vacío.
4. Quieres pedir una pista sin admitir derrota.
5. Terminaste y quieres mencionar un caso límite pendiente.

**Ejercicio 22F — Corrige.**
1. *I have 5 years of experience working as developer.*
2. *Actually I work in a fintech company.* *(quieres decir "actualmente")*
3. *I am agree with that approach.*
4. *Explain me the requirements please.*
5. *I have experience in Python since 3 years.*
6. *We were 5 people in the team.*
7. *I made a master in computer science.*
8. *My last job I was responsible of the API.*
9. *Can you repeat, please? I didn't listen you.*
10. *In my actual company we use microservices.*

**Ejercicio 22G — Traduce.**
1. Para dar contexto, hace un año estaba en un equipo de cinco personas manteniendo el servicio de pagos.
2. Mi preocupación era que ya teníamos cuatro responsabilidades distintas en ese archivo.
3. Así que lo que hice fue pasar una tarde escribiendo una pequeña prueba de concepto.
4. El resultado fue que optamos por el adaptador; nos costó unos dos días más al principio.
5. Lo que me llevé de ahí es que una prueba de concepto que funciona mueve una conversación más rápido que una discusión.
6. Antes de empezar, ¿puedo aclarar un par de cosas?
7. ¿Qué debería pasar si la lista está vacía?
8. Mi primera idea es una solución de fuerza bruta, y luego busco optimizarla.
9. Eso es tiempo lineal y espacio constante.
10. Vamos a hacer unos números aproximados a ojo.

### 8 · Challenge

**Reto 52.** Escribe y memoriza **cinco** historias STAR (desacuerdo, error en producción, plazo ajustado, feedback difícil, proyecto del que estás orgulloso). Máximo 90 segundos cada una, con un número medible en el Result. Grábalas y cronométralas.

**Reto 53 (oral, 20 min).** Coge un problema de LeetCode de nivel medio que ya sepas resolver y resuélvelo **hablando en inglés todo el tiempo**, sin silencios de más de tres segundos. Graba. Escúchalo y cuenta los silencios y las veces que dices *"eeeh"* en lugar de un filler inglés.

**Reto 54 (oral, 30 min).** *"Design a URL shortener"* o *"Design a rate limiter"*. Recorre las cinco fases en voz alta. Escríbete de antemano solo las frases de apertura de cada fase; el resto improvisado.

**Reto 55.** Prepara siete preguntas para el entrevistador, específicas de la empresa a la que quieres entrar, y una respuesta preparada para *"Is there anything about my background that gives you pause?"*.

### 9 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| *In my actual company…* | In my **current** company | *actual* = "real, verdadero" |
| *Actually I work at…* | **Currently** I work at… | *actually* = "en realidad" |
| *I have experience since 3 years.* | I've **had** … **for** 3 years | |
| *I made a master's.* | I **did / took** a master's | |
| *I am responsible of…* | responsible **for** | |
| *We were 5 people in the team.* | There were 5 **of us on** the team | |
| *Explain me the problem.* | Explain **the problem to me** | |
| *I didn't listen you.* | I didn't **hear** you | |
| *working as developer* | as **a** developer | artículo obligatorio |
| *I assist to meetings.* | I **attend** meetings | |
| *My English is not very good, sorry.* | *I'll ask if I need anything repeated.* | nunca te disculpes por tu inglés |
| *Sorry for my bad English.* | (elimínalo) | resta credibilidad sin ganar nada |

```{=openxml}
<w:p><w:r><w:br w:type="page"/></w:r></w:p>
```


## UNIT 23 — Negotiating: salary, scope and deadlines

**Nivel:** B2.4 · **Escenario:** una oferta que quieres mejorar, y un product manager que quiere meter tres features más en el mismo sprint.
**Meta comunicativa:** defender tu posición sin romper la relación.
**Lengua:** lenguaje diplomático · concesiones condicionales · *push back* · silencio y anclaje.

### 1 · Input — la oferta

> **RECRUITER:** So we'd like to move forward. The offer is 78 for base, plus the standard equity package and a 10% target bonus.
>
> **YOU:** Thanks — I'm really pleased to hear that, and I'm genuinely excited about the team. **Can I be upfront with you about the number?** Based on what I've seen for senior backend roles with distributed systems experience in this market, **I was expecting something closer to 92.** **Is there any flexibility on the base?**
>
> **RECRUITER:** 92 would be at the very top of the band for this level. I might be able to get to 84.
>
> **YOU:** I appreciate you looking into it. **Let me be clear that base is the piece that matters most to me** — equity is harder for me to value. **If you could get to 88, I'd sign today.** And **if the band genuinely caps out below that**, I'd want to understand what the path to the next level looks like, and over what timeframe.
>
> **RECRUITER:** Let me take 88 to the hiring manager. I can't promise anything.
>
> **YOU:** That's all I'm asking. **One other thing while we're here** — I've got two weeks of holiday already booked in September. **Would that be a problem?**

### 2 · Notice — la anatomía de una negociación en inglés

| Movimiento | Lenguaje |
|---|---|
| **1. Agradecer y mostrar entusiasmo** (nunca lo omitas) | *Thanks — I'm really pleased. I'm genuinely excited about the team.* |
| **2. Pedir permiso para ser directo** | *Can I be upfront with you?* / *Can I be direct?* / *Let me be transparent about where I am.* |
| **3. Anclar con una cifra justificada** | *Based on X, I was expecting something closer to Y.* |
| **4. Preguntar por el margen** | *Is there any flexibility on the base?* |
| **5. Declarar tu prioridad** | *Base is the piece that matters most to me.* |
| **6. Conceder condicionalmente** | *If you could get to 88, I'd sign today.* |
| **7. Plan B si no hay margen** | *If the band caps out below that, I'd want to understand…* |
| **8. Agrupar el resto** | *One other thing while we're here…* |
| **9. Dejar espacio** | *Take your time. / Let me know when you've had a chance to look.* |

**Frases de anclaje (con justificación, que es lo que las hace funcionar):**

| Chunk |
|---|
| *Based on what I've seen in this market, I was expecting closer to X.* |
| *My current total comp is X, so I'd need to see a meaningful step up.* |
| *I've got another process at a similar stage, and the range there is X.* |
| *For the level of ownership described, X is where I'd expect to be.* |
| *I'd want the number to reflect the on-call component.* |

**Frases de concesión condicional** (el núcleo técnico de negociar en inglés — *"si tú X, yo Y"*):

| Chunk |
|---|
| *If you could get to X, I'd sign today.* |
| *If the base is fixed, could we look at the signing bonus instead?* |
| *I could be flexible on start date if there's movement on base.* |
| *I'd be happy with X provided the review is brought forward to six months.* |
| *That works for me as long as we can revisit it at the six-month mark.* |
| *I could live with X if the equity vests on a one-year cliff.* |

> ⚠️ **Nunca concedas sin condición.** ❌ *"OK, 84 is fine."* → has regalado 4.000. ✅ *"I could work with 84 if we bring the salary review forward to six months."*

**Cuando no hay margen — palancas alternativas:**

| Palanca | Frase |
|---|---|
| Signing bonus | *Is there room in the signing bonus?* |
| Equity | *Could we look at the equity grant instead?* |
| Revisión anticipada | *Could we agree a review at six months rather than twelve?* |
| Nivel | *Would you consider me at the next level up?* |
| Título | *Is the title negotiable?* |
| Remoto / días | *Would four days remote be possible?* |
| Vacaciones | *Is there flexibility on holiday allowance?* |
| Formación | *Is there a learning and conference budget?* |
| Fecha de inicio | *I'd need to give a month's notice.* |

**Chunks para ganar tiempo (no aceptes nunca en el momento):**

| Chunk |
|---|
| *Thanks — could I have a couple of days to look at the whole package?* |
| *I'd like to run it past my partner before I commit.* |
| *Could you send that over in writing?* |
| *When do you need an answer by?* |
| *I don't want to give you a rushed answer.* |

> **Nota cultural:** el silencio negocia. Después de una cifra, deja dos segundos de pausa antes de responder. Los hispanohablantes tendemos a rellenar ese silencio y con ello a debilitar la posición. Y **nunca** des tu cifra actual antes de que ellos den la suya: *"I'd rather not anchor on my current number — what's the range for the role?"*

### 3 · Notice — negociar alcance y plazos

Esta es la negociación diaria y la que más importa para tu vida. El principio: **no digas "no" — di "sí, y esto es lo que cuesta"**.

| Registro | Frase |
|---|---|
| **Nunca** | ❌ *It's impossible.* ❌ *No, we can't.* ❌ *That's not my job.* |
| **En su lugar** | ✅ *We can do that. What I'd need to move out of the sprint to fit it in is X.* |

**Estructura de la negociación de alcance:**

| Paso | Lenguaje |
|---|---|
| **Reconocer** | *I can see why that's a priority.* / *That makes sense from a customer point of view.* |
| **Exponer la restricción** | *The constraint we're up against is capacity, not willingness.* |
| **Hacer visible el trade-off** | *We've got room for two of the three. Which two matter most?* / *Something has to come out. Your call on which.* |
| **Ofrecer opciones, no un no** | *There are three ways we could play this: cut scope, move the date, or ship it behind a flag.* |
| **Devolver la decisión** | *I'm happy either way — it's a product call, not an engineering one.* |
| **Confirmar por escrito** | *Just to confirm what we agreed: X is in, Y moves to next sprint.* |

**Chunks para discrepar con firmeza sin agresividad (*pushing back*):**

| Chunk | Fuerza |
|---|---|
| *I'd push back on that a little.* | suave |
| *I'm not sure that follows.* | suave |
| *Can I offer a different read on that?* | suave |
| *I hear you, but I don't think that gets us there.* | media |
| *I want to flag a risk with that plan.* | media |
| *I'd be uncomfortable committing to that date.* | media |
| *I don't think that's realistic, and I'd rather say so now than in three weeks.* | firme |
| *I can't commit to that. What I can commit to is X.* | firme |
| *I'd want that decision documented, because I don't agree with it.* | muy firme, formal |
| *I'll do it, but I want to be on record that I think it's the wrong call.* | *disagree and commit* |

> ***Disagree and commit*** es un concepto muy anglosajón: expresas el desacuerdo una vez, con claridad, y después ejecutas al 100% sin sabotear ni recordarlo. La frase canónica: *"I've said what I think. The decision's yours, and I'm behind it."*

**Chunks para proteger tu tiempo:**

| Chunk |
|---|
| *I've got capacity for one of those this week. Which one?* |
| *I can pick that up on Thursday at the earliest.* |
| *That's going to need a bigger conversation than Slack.* |
| *Can we put that in the backlog and prioritise it properly?* |
| *If that's urgent, something else has to give.* |
| *I'd rather do one of them well than three badly.* |
| *Let's timebox it to a day and see where we get to.* |
| *I'm going to say no to this one so I can finish X.* |

### 4 · Notice — decir "no" en inglés

| Español (directo) | Inglés (equivalente funcional) |
|---|---|
| No puedo. | *I won't be able to, I'm afraid.* |
| No estoy de acuerdo. | *I see it differently.* |
| Eso está mal. | *I don't think that's quite right.* |
| Es imposible. | *I can't see a way to do that by Friday.* |
| No es mi trabajo. | *That's outside what I'm able to pick up — the right person would be X.* |
| No tengo tiempo. | *I don't have the capacity this sprint.* |
| Ya te lo dije. | *As I mentioned earlier, …* |
| Te equivocas. | *I think there might be a misunderstanding.* |
| No. | *Not this time, I'm afraid.* |

> La regla general: el inglés profesional **nunca niega el hecho, niega la posibilidad**. En lugar de *"eso es falso"*, dices *"I'm not sure that's the case"*. Esto no es hipocresía: es un protocolo que permite a la otra persona rectificar sin perder la cara, lo cual hace más probable que rectifique.

### 5 · Sound

**Foco:** la entonación lo es todo en la negociación. La misma frase puede ser colaborativa u hostil.

| Frase | ↗ (colaborativo) | ↘ (definitivo) |
|---|---|---|
| *Is there any flexibility on the base?* | ↗ correcto aquí | ↘ suena a exigencia |
| *I can't commit to that.* | ↗ suena inseguro | ↘ correcto aquí |
| *If you could get to 88, I'd sign today.* | ↗ en *88*, ↘ en *today* | |
| *That's all I'm asking.* | | ↘ |

**Números en voz alta:** practica decir cifras con confianza y sin subir el tono al final: *"ninety-two"* ↘. Un número dicho con entonación ascendente suena a pregunta y se negocia a la baja.

**Práctica de pausa:** graba esta secuencia dejando **dos segundos completos** de silencio donde está marcado:
> *"I might be able to get to 84."* — [dos segundos] — *"I appreciate you looking into it."*

Contar los dos segundos resulta físicamente incómodo. Ese es el ejercicio.

### 6 · Practice

**Ejercicio 23A — Convierte cada concesión en concesión condicional.**
1. *OK, 84 is fine.*
2. *Sure, I can start on the 1st.*
3. *Fine, we'll add the third feature.*
4. *Alright, I'll take it on.*

**Ejercicio 23B — Reformula sin decir "no".**
1. *It's impossible to do all three by Friday.*
2. *No, that's not my job.*
3. *I don't have time.*
4. *You're wrong about the estimate.*
5. *I already told you that.*

**Ejercicio 23C — Ordena una negociación de oferta.** Coloca en orden lógico: *(a) Is there any flexibility on the base? (b) Thanks — I'm really pleased. (c) If you could get to 88, I'd sign today. (d) Can I be upfront about the number? (e) Based on this market, I was expecting closer to 92. (f) One other thing while we're here…*

**Ejercicio 23D — Elige la palanca alternativa.** El base está bloqueado. Escribe una frase para cada palanca: signing bonus, revisión a 6 meses, nivel, remoto, vacaciones.

**Ejercicio 23E — Gradúa el pushback** de más suave a más firme: *(a) I can't commit to that. (b) I'd push back on that a little. (c) I want to flag a risk with that plan. (d) I'll do it, but I want to be on record that I think it's the wrong call. (e) I'm not sure that follows.*

**Ejercicio 23F — Corrige.**
1. *I want that you increase the salary.*
2. *Is possible to negotiate the base?*
3. *I am agree with 84 if you move the review.*
4. *I have a compromise on Friday.* *(quieres decir "un compromiso/una cita")*
5. *Sorry, but it's impossible.*
6. *I will can start in September.*
7. *Let me to think about it.*
8. *I expect that you understand my position.*
9. *We must to decide today.*
10. *I don't have capacity for do it.*

**Ejercicio 23G — Traduce.**
1. Gracias, me alegra oírlo, y me entusiasma el equipo de verdad.
2. ¿Puedo ser franco contigo sobre la cifra?
3. Por lo que he visto en este mercado, esperaba algo más cercano a 92.
4. ¿Hay algún margen en el salario base?
5. Te agradezco que lo consultes.
6. Que quede claro que el base es lo que más me importa.
7. Si pudieras llegar a 88, firmaría hoy mismo.
8. Y si la banda realmente no llega a eso, querría entender cuál es el camino al siguiente nivel.
9. Podemos hacerlo. Lo que necesitaría sacar del sprint para que quepa es X.
10. Tenemos sitio para dos de las tres. ¿Cuáles dos importan más?

### 7 · Challenge

**Reto 56 (oral, 5 min).** Ensaya la negociación completa del Input en voz alta, tú haciendo los dos papeles, con las pausas de dos segundos incluidas. Grábate. Escucha si tus números suenan con entonación descendente.

**Reto 57.** Escribe el correo de seguimiento tras una llamada de oferta: agradece, resume lo hablado, confirma la petición por escrito, y da una fecha para tu respuesta. Máximo 120 palabras.

**Reto 58 (escrito).** Un PM te pide meter tres features en un sprint donde caben dos. Escribe tu respuesta de Slack: reconoce la prioridad, expón la restricción, ofrece tres opciones, devuelve la decisión. Máximo 90 palabras y **sin** la palabra *no*.

**Reto 59 (oral).** Practica *disagree and commit*: expresa un desacuerdo técnico real en tres frases y cierra comprometiéndote a ejecutar la decisión contraria.

### 8 · Trampas del hispanohablante

| Error | Correcto | Nota |
|---|---|---|
| *I want that you increase it.* | I'd like **you to** increase it | nunca *want that* |
| *Is possible to negotiate?* | **Is it** possible…? | |
| *I have a compromise at 5.* | I have a **commitment / a meeting** | *compromise* = "acuerdo con concesiones" |
| *I will can start…* | I'**ll be able to** start | dos modales nunca juntos |
| *Let me to think.* | Let me **think** | |
| *We must to decide.* | We must **decide** | |
| *I expect that you understand.* | I **hope** you'll understand | *expect* = "esperar/exigir" |
| *capacity for do it* | capacity **to do** it | |
| *Sorry, it's impossible.* | I can't see a way to… | evita *impossible* |
| *I demand…* | I'd like… / I'd need… | *demand* es muy agresivo |
| *It's not fair.* | *I don't think that reflects the level of the role.* | |
| *My salary is very low.* | *I'd need the number to reflect X.* | no argumentes desde la carencia |
